Status: in progress
Milestone: **Bots on the simulation** (`games/shooters_edge/bots`, on `engine/ai`, after M18o and M18f): a bot is a client of the server like any player, its only output a usercmd a frame through the same move and the same game program, so it can do nothing a player cannot and every improvement to the simulation is one to the bots; navigation generated from the map on `ai/path` (where a player can walk, jump, fall, swim and ride), sight and hearing on `ai/sight` over the hull trace, aim and movement on `ai/hands` with skill profiles, item timing and fights chosen on `ai/choose`, the strafe-jump and the rocket jump as moves the bot plans; every decision recorded for `ge why`, humanness measured against people's input logs with `ai/human`; `ge bot` to run, compare and sweep them
Exit: Eight bots play a match on a free map for ten minutes with a golden digest; their moves and aim pass the humanness bands; a change to a bot shows its before and after in frags, deaths, items and humanness
Labs: `se-nav`, `se-pilot`, `se-senses`, `se-bots`, `se-team`, `se-server`, `se-moves`, `se-hop`, `se-kills`, `se-rj`, `humanness`

After M18o (the loop) and M18f (the server's client slots): bots that play through the
simulation exactly as a player does, a usercmd a frame and nothing else, on `engine/ai`'s paths,
sight, hands and choosing, with every decision recorded for `ge why` and humanness measured
against people's input logs. The design is in `docs/shooters-edge.md` § Playing together.

Built in six steps, each its own pull request:

- **M18r1, navigation proven by the move** (`plugins/shooter/nav.hpp`): **done.** The floor
  sampled on a 32-unit grid off the map's own hulls, every link from a node proven by running
  `player_move` from it (walk, drop, or a jump on the move at full, half or a quarter speed, one
  flight with room to run up), movers left out and tagged on the nodes and links they close, the
  quickest route on `ai/path`'s A* over the proofs' times. Held by `tests/shooter_nav_test.cpp`
  on the nav course (`games/shooters_edge/sim/nav_course.hpp`: stairs, a crate, a pit, a ramp and
  a ramp too steep to stand on) and the test arena's doorway, its bits by
  `tests/golden/shooter_nav.txt`; pictured by the `se-nav` lab. A standing jump is not a kind:
  its 30 u/s of air control carries a player less than the 16 units its box needs to get over an
  edge, so it reaches nothing a moving jump does not. Riding a mover (a plat) waits for M18r2,
  which reads the mover's place at run time.
- **M18r2, the pilot** (`plugins/shooter/pilot.hpp`): **done.** A route played one usercmd at a
  time through `ai/hands`, the view's yaw and the movement axes on the protocol's grid, the jump a
  button: pure pursuit along the level walks, each jump link run at from a run-up point behind its
  start and pressed on its line at its proven speed, the strafe jump on long straight stretches
  (the view along the velocity, the side axis full), waiting stepped clear of a door that closes a
  link (and barring it after `wait_s`), riding the arena's plat (the graph now places a ridden
  mover at its travel's end and proves the ways off its top there), the stuck and lost checks
  planning again. Every decision on `ai::Why`. On the server it fills a bot slot through the
  command-source seam (`pilot_source`). Held by `tests/shooter_pilot_test.cpp` on the nav course
  (every route in about its proven time, human hands arrive, the strafe faster than a walk) and the
  arena (through the door, up the plat, the door barred, with both hands), its bits by
  `tests/golden/shooter_pilot.txt`; pictured by `se-pilot`. Open: human hands loop wide at
  corners (the pilot does not yet slow for one) and their forward axis reverses about 135 times a
  minute against a person's 120, both for a later pass with M18r5's `ge bot compare`; the
  pitch waits for aim (M18r3).
- **M18r3, senses and aim** (`plugins/shooter/senses.hpp`, `aim.hpp`, `bot.hpp`): **done.**
  A bot knows only what its client is sent (`client_view`: every entity with a model, every other
  player, the frame's sounds within `kHearAt / attenuation`; the box-brush maps have no PVS, so
  nothing is cut yet), sees a player inside its view's cone with a clear `trace_move` line to the
  head or the middle (a shut door hides), hears the sounds it is sent, and remembers both on
  `ai/memory`, forgetting after six seconds. It aims on two `ai/hands` axes (yaw and pitch) under
  four skill profiles (perfect, expert, average, novice: delay, wander, settle and slew), leading
  the target by its seen velocity, and keeps running its pilot's route while it looks (the movement
  axes turned under the aim's view, `turn_axes`). Every sense change and the aim's wanted angles on
  `ai::Why`. Held by `tests/shooter_senses_test.cpp` (what is sent, the cone, the door and the
  wall, hearing's reach, the median error ordered by skill on two seeds, a human reaction, the
  route kept), its bits by `tests/golden/shooter_senses.txt`; pictured by `se-senses`. Open: the
  native rules make sounds only on fire, so hearing is unit-tested and waits for M18r4's fights on
  the baseline mod to be heard in play; the pilot's strafe is dropped while aiming.
- **M18r4, fights and items** on the game's scripted baseline mod (M18d)
  (`games/shooters_edge/bots/fighter.hpp`, `fight_run.hpp`): **done, but for the rocket jump.**
  A fighter is the M18r3 bot choosing, on `ai/choose`, from what a client has: its own stats as
  the server sends them, the map's items off the map's entity text (a client loads the map), each
  one timed from the moment it stops being sent (20 s, a weapon 30 s), and the others through its
  senses. Every tenth of a second it picks its mode (`ai::enter`: roam, fight, chase, retreat), its
  goal (`ai::pick_best`: each item's worth to it now over the time to have it, the goal held
  against a near tie) and its weapon (`ai::pick_best` by range, sent as the impulse); every command
  whether to fire (the view within the target's width at its range of the aim, in range; rockets
  and grenades at the feet, a projectile led by its flight). The senses now drop a dead player (the
  protocol's dead flag). `run_fight` plays four fighters on the baseline mod compiled from its
  sources over the arena; held by `tests/shooters_edge_fight_test.cpp` (every fighter scores, dies,
  picks up, switches and holds a better weapon, all four modes, no fault, the expert pair outscoring
  the novice pair on two seeds), its bits by `tests/golden/shooters_edge_fight.txt`; pictured by
  `se-bots`. Measured: about 27 frags a minute among four; over seeds 1 to 3 the experts took 99
  frags to the novices' 67. Open: the rocket jump (**M18r4b**, a move the pilot plans through the
  nav's proofs with the blast's push); items are taken about 30 times in two minutes, fewer than
  players manage, since most are worth nothing to a full-health player and the fights keep
  breaking the route.
- **M18r7, the team game** (`games/shooters_edge/bots/team.hpp`, `net/qw_team.hpp`):
  **first pass done.** Two teams of four fighters, each at the controls of a classic client of
  our server over the simulated network, play the baseline mod with `teamplay 1` (no damage to a
  teammate, a backpack with the victim's ammunition and weapon on every death) while the server
  records the match as a multiview demo. A team plans every half second, the way a team plays:
  its strongest player holds the map's best armour and the area round it (a leash, not a post),
  the two nearest the quad rush it from twelve seconds before it is due, whoever has the quad
  carries it to the enemy last called out, a weak player falls back to the health near the held
  area, a teammate's claim leaves an item to him, a backpack in view is taken when ammunition is
  short, and no one fires with a teammate within 40 units of the line. The fighters now read the
  map's buttons (a door's button touched or shot first, the button pressed from the side the line
  meets it), reach every item only where a route leads from where they stand, and walk the last
  step onto an item; the nav graph gains the map's teleporters (`nav_add_links`), its plats at
  their rest, its pools closed to entry (`nav_close_entries`), and a way out of the water
  (`plugins/shooter/swim.hpp`: a breadth-first field over the wet cells to the dry nodes, swum
  with the view pitched and jump held), and a dive into a pocket only the water reaches (the
  same field built from the pocket's side: a fighter wanting an item there walks to the pool's
  edge and swims in). Held by `tests/shooters_edge_team_test.cpp` (a minute on the fixture halls:
  both teams score, no team kill, every role, the recording opens with all eight players, a match
  replays to the bit; `tests/golden/shooters_edge_team.txt`) and `tests/shooter_swim_test.cpp`;
  pictured by `se-team`, which plays the shareware pack's first episode's second map with
  `--basedir`. Measured on that map, ten minutes: 79 frags (39 to 40), ten quads taken by both
  teams, the rocket launcher dived for and held by four players, a 22 MB recording. The fight
  test now sums the experts' frags over four seeds (one seed is a coin toss): 140 to the novices'
  99. Open: the frag rate is about half of people's (the aim wastes shots at range with the
  scattergun), the armour behind the shot button is not yet taken on that map, and deaths exceed
  frags (falls and drowning count against the dead).
- **M18r8, team talk and map knowledge** (`games/shooters_edge/bots/knowledge.hpp`, `team.hpp`):
  **done.** A fighter knows the map's items by its skill: an expert knows every item and times
  the key ones (the best armours, the mega health, the rocket launcher, the quad, the shield) once
  he has seen one taken; an average player knows every item but times only the small ones, so a
  key item he saw go is guessed back late; a novice knows none at the start, goes for what he
  sees, and recalls an item only after three separate sightings (24 of 57 by the end of ten
  minutes on e1m2). Belief comes from sight alone. A team talks over `say_team` (the server now
  sends a team's words to its own, and the recording keeps them): "quad in 10, get quad!" from
  whoever timed it, "going quad", "have quad!", "lost quad", "enemy at RA" (a sighting is the
  team's only once called out), "need help" (a strong free member answers), "took RA", "RA in
  20". A walk along a pool's floor turns up and out after six seconds under, and a dive once in
  the water is swum to its end. Measured on e1m2, ten minutes: 79 frags (43 to 36), no drowning
  (13 before), about 18 team messages a player. Open: the rocket launcher's pocket is reached
  only by a dive whose last metre (out of the water onto its ledge) fails, and the armour behind
  the shot button sits in an alcove no route proves a way into.
- **M18r9, minds of their own, team message keys and place names** (`bots/mind.hpp`,
  `bots/talk.hpp`, `plugins/classic/locs.hpp`): **done.** No shared planner: each bot reads the
  team lines its own client is sent (a person's too) and plans its role alone. The messages are a
  team game's message keys (report, took, safe, lost, help, coming, point, enemy, have, get,
  over, timers), read back in the spellings people type. Reading by skill: an expert reads every
  line, an average player about half of the lines said once and nearly every repeat, a novice one
  in six and nothing in a fight; timers are exact for an expert and a few seconds out for an
  average player. The opening takes on the nearest of the launcher, the best armour (the mega
  health on a map whose armour no way reaches) and the quad, called `coming X`, then `safe [X]`;
  the quad is called by whoever timed it and repeated while no mate is near it; an item of no use
  to its finder, or the carrier's due, is pointed at for a teammate. Place names come from the
  game folder's `locs/<map>.loc`, or off the map's items. Measured on e1m2 with its community
  place names, ten minutes: 103 frags (51 to 52), one drowning, about 39 team messages a player,
  980 lines read and 892 missed across the eight. A dive that cannot finish is given up: in the
  water 3 s longer than its planned swim, or short of air with the way back shorter than the way
  on, it turns back and that dive is not tried for 30 s. Over ten seeds on e1m2 the bots had
  spent 2990 s in the water on dives (bobbing under the launcher's ledge, up to 821 s a match);
  with the give-up 96 s, and 910 frags against 789. Open: two matches in ten jam players inside
  each other at the teleporter's exit by the launcher (800 368 312), with or without the give-up:
  the mod's teleporter does not kill whoever stands on the exit.
- **M18r10, ears, a map of the team, area keeping and buttons** (`plugins/shooter/hearing.hpp`,
  `bots/ears.hpp`, `bots/spam.hpp`, `bots/buttons.hpp`): **done.** Sounds are heard by skill (an
  expert everything and every sample's meaning, the distance to a tenth; an average player close
  and unique sounds; a novice close ones only, no meanings); a pickup heard starts its item's timer
  and places its taker, a spawn places a fresh enemy. The mega health is timed only by an expert
  who took it, from his own health back at 100. An expert keeps teammates' places for ten seconds
  and reads a key area none of them is in as lost; an average player goes by `lost` lines. An
  expert holder with 20+ rockets fires at teleporter exits and power spawns while his item is
  away. Experts rate key items by scarcity on the map. Help only over a key area being lost;
  novices say only their status. Buttons come off the map's entities (shot when health is over 0):
  dm2's 200 armour, e1m2's 150, cmt4's pentagram door and cmt3's bridge are found; novices poke at
  any button. The baseline mod plays the spawn's fog sound and telefrags on a taken spawn point.
  Measured on e1m2 (ten minutes, its community place names): 11.5 frags a minute, 1048 sounds
  taken in, 65 items heard taken, 27 spawns heard. Open: dm4's bots burn in its lava, the 4v4's
  rocket spam and retakes are rare where launchers are scarce, the dm2 graph leaves most items
  unrouted. Next: M18r11, prediction and hit rates (shots at heard enemies, tracers corrected by
  the miss, grenade bounces, the hit-rate bands per weapon and skill).
- **M18r11a, temperaments** (`plugins/shooter/temper.hpp`, `bots/temper.hpp`): **done.**
  Aggressive, balanced and coward, each taking a fight by might against might (body times arms) and
  the skill's edge; the enemy's might read off what is heard of him (weapons fired, armour, mega
  and quad taken, pain), fading in 30 s. Outmatched bots turn for items away from the enemy or
  their mates; aggressive bots never hold or give items up. Measured on the arena: aggressive
  novices die about 25% more than cowardly ones and fight over four times their share; experts
  aggressive, balanced and coward made 49, 41 and 33 frags. Next: the personality traits (each
  1 to 10, novice to expert as presets over them), then M18r11's prediction and hit rates.
- **M18r11b, personalities** (`plugins/shooter/traits.hpp`): **done.** Fifteen traits from 1 to
  10 (aim per weapon, reaction, timing, knowledge, camping, discipline, moves, hearing,
  communication, teamplay, aggression), the skills and temperaments presets over them that play
  bit for bit as before (every golden unchanged); the aim's hands taken up anew per weapon. Labs
  take `--traits`. Open: camping only drives the expert holder's rockets at exits (the camp spots
  themselves wait for the kill map's gold spots); discipline only the rash chase.
- **M18r11c, reactions per sighting** (`plugins/shooter/aim.hpp` `draw_reaction`, `bot.hpp`):
  **done.** A reaction drawn per sighting, right-skewed (trait 8 round 160 ms, 5 round 230, 2
  round 300), the view held and no shot until it is over; the hands' lag cut to the tracking lag
  inside it; half the draw when pre-aimed (the only way under 100 ms); a heard enemy chased 100 to
  200 ms later than a seen one. On 4on4 e1m2 (three seeds) the drawn means came out 157, 220 and
  295 ms and the match's frags a minute rose from 6.9 to 9.6. Next: hit rates per weapon held to
  the division bands, prediction, grenade bounces, camp spots from the kill map.
- **M18r11d, hit rates per weapon** (`games/shooters_edge/net/qw_hits.hpp`): **measured,
  first tuning in.** Shots off ammunition spent, hits off blows taken, per player and per skill in
  `se-team`, and weapon drills (`--drill=<weapon>`: every player holds only it). The first reading
  (5 to 29% for an expert's double) was mostly the counter's: the ammunition a respawn empties had
  been read as shots. With that fixed, the trigger reading the view the command carries (a swim or
  a button had turned it off the aim), the arena's teleporters telefragging (players had stacked
  inside each other at a destination and shot each other unharmed for whole matches), a finger as
  loose as the aim trait, the double kept within 350 units and close rockets aimed at the middle,
  dm6 drills over two seeds read (novice / average / expert): the double 23 / 39 / 44 (bands 15–25
  / 25–35 / 35–45), the arc 24 / 36 / 44 (15–25 / 28–35 / 38–45), a rocket's direct hit 16 / 18 /
  19 (10–18 / 22–28 / 30–35), its blast 36 / 36 / 34 (12–15 / 15–20 / 18–25), grenades 30 / 32 /
  33 (2–5 / 5–10 / 8–15). Next: prediction (the rockets' direct hits by skill), grenades and
  rockets fired blind into rooms and spawns (the blast and the grenades' rates come down with the
  shots no one sees land), camp spots from the kill map; on e1m2, dm2 and dm3 the nav graph leaves
  key items out of reach.
- **M18r12, expert bots bunny hop like a pro** (`plugins/shooter/hops.hpp`, `pilot.hpp`):
  **done.** A recorded pro's movement read off a multiview demo (`read_hops`: velocity, ground,
  chained jumps, the air's turn against the most the move allows, the view's lag), and the
  pilot's HOP mode flying the same way: the side key alone, the view led on the velocity by the
  turn it can make, the side switched mid-air toward the route, the jump on landing, a wall on the
  arc ahead flipping the side. Expert and perfect fighters hop (practised hands); average ones
  hop the simple way (forward and the side key of the turn, steady hands: their speed kept on a
  straight, gained in curves); novices walk.
  Held by `tests/shooter_hops_test.cpp` (an ideal hopper reads chained, turning at the most with no
  lag; a walker no jumps; cuts; the pilot hops the course's straight faster than it walks), the
  fighters' and the team's goldens rewritten for it (experts 124 frags to novices' 86 over seeds
  1 to 4). Labs `se-moves` and `se-hop` (wireframes from above, at an angle and inside, the
  chase GIF). Measured on the demo's map: the pro jumps 30 a minute, his speed's p90 466; the
  expert hop's p90 450 against the walk's 320, his trips in about 1.2x his time against the
  walk's 1.6x; five of his eight longest trips flagged as tricks or shortcuts.
  Open: the human-handed hop loses speed (the hands' spring lags the turn); the simple hop has no
  look ahead for walls (giving it the expert's made it slower); no bot yet hops through a fight.
- **M18r13, where kills happen on a map, from many demos** (`client/demo_scan.hpp`, lab
  `se-kills`): **done.** The demo scan keeps where each killer and victim stood, the rocket or
  grenade that did it, and every rocket and grenade flown with its likely shooter (the player
  nearest where it first shows). The lab reads a folder of one map's recordings and draws every
  kill and flight over the wireframe as an added-up density, picks out the GOLD SHOTS (the same
  pair of cubes again and again) with their xyz, filters by weapon, and writes `kills.tsv` and
  `gold.tsv` for a bot's tables. Held by `tests/shooters_edge_demo_highlights_test.cpp`.
  Measured on 300 recent ztndm3 games from the public game list (51 hours, read in 8 minutes on
  four threads): 29,575 kills, 54% rockets and 39% the beam; 176 gold shots hold 11% of them;
  172,181 rockets and grenades, two in three with a shooter found; the top rocket lane was flown
  947 times in 270 of the 300 games; 7,311 rockets went off at the feet (rocket jumps).
  Next: the bots read `gold.tsv` (a spot to pre-aim, a lane to fire a rocket down blind) in the
  team bots' aim (M18r11).
- **M18r14, bots on an existing server**: a team of our bots joins a live classic server (an
  existing server running the common match mod, built beside the checkout and never in the tree)
  and plays a 4on4 against people, so the bots are tested where it matters and every match leaves
  data to learn from. One headless process runs a whole team of bots, each its own classic client
  over UDP on the wall clock; two processes can run the two teams of a bots-only match, neither
  knowing the other. Each bot knows only what its own client is sent and what its teammates say
  on `say_team`; the process describes its team's game (the score and its trend, each area, each
  member) from its bots' beliefs without feeding anything back into them. The design is in
  `docs/shooters-edge.md` § Playing together, "Bots on an existing server". In five steps, each
  its own pull request:
  - **M18r14a, the client's world** (`net/qw_mirror.hpp`): **done.** The fighter's world rebuilt from
    what its client was sent (its own player and stats, the other players' places and dead flags,
    the entities of the newest frame, the frame's sounds, the frags, the prints), so the fighter
    reads no server at all; `run_team_match` plays on it beside the server-fed run, and every
    fact the server-fed fighter used that a client is never sent (another player's health, an
    item outside the client's visibility set read as taken) is found and closed. Held by a test
    (both runs score, no team kill, the mirror equal to the server wherever the client is sent
    it) and a golden for the client-fed run. Landed: the mirror, `run_team_match`'s `client_fed`
    and `se-team --client-fed=1`; held by `tests/shooters_edge_qw_mirror_test.cpp` (both teams
    score on the halls, no team kill, every fighter on its mirror, its own place within 1.2 units of
    the server's on average). What it found: the prediction set the water level only while it ran a
    command, so on a LAN (no move unacknowledged) a bot under water read as dry and drowned
    (`water_level` in `plugins/shooter/pmove.hpp`, read after the prediction); our server tested a
    brush entity's visibility at its origin (a door's is its model's own, the map's 0 0 0), so no
    door was ever sent and client-fed bots waited at doors they believed shut (39 waits a bot to
    about 2 now, 1 dive to 49; `net/qw_frame.cpp` tests the box); a mover the client was not
    sent is taken as the nav planned it (`plugins/shooter/pilot.cpp`). The server-fed goldens are
    unchanged. Open: a golden for the client-fed run.
  - **M18r14b, the bot team process** (`sebots`): **done.** N fighters on N clients over UDP
    against `--server=<host:port>`, paced by the wall clock as the window's remote client is; the
    map loaded from the player's own packs by the name serverdata carries and its nav built; the
    match mod's lines obeyed (the team and colours it stuffs, `ready` and `break` on the owner's
    word or when every person is ready, the countdown, the map's change and the reconnect); a
    match folder written (each bot's client recording: every packet read and command sent, its
    decisions, the team's lines). `se-interop --bots=4` drives it against a live server. Landed:
    `net/qw_bot_team.hpp` (the team, its console, a death line per death), `net/bots_main.cpp`
    (`sebots`), the window's bot client (`shooters_edge --bots=N --server=...`, the
    console's lines no command names going to the bots through `Console::set_unknown`), the place
    names from a client's folder (`locs/<map>.loc`); held by `tests/shooters_edge_bot_team_test.cpp`.
    Measured on an existing server (mvdsv with ktx, e1m2, 4on4 of our bots, 10 minutes): both
    teams join, ready, play the match to its end and the server records it; 46 to 40, 25 of about
    210 deaths drowned before the water level's fix. Open: `se-interop --bots`.
  - **M18r14c, the narrator** (`bots/narrator.hpp`): **first pass in**: the team's report
    (`bot_team_report`: the score, each bot's health, armour, mode, role, goal and place) every
    `--report` seconds and on `report`, and the team's picture (`net/qw_team_view.hpp`: the map,
    each bot's route, goal and believed enemies, the item timers, the enemies the team's clients were
    sent together, the plans) drawn in the window and recorded as a video. Open: the process's view of its own team, read off
    its bots' beliefs and the public scores and never written back: the score and its trend; each
    area by its place name (held, lost, contested, last seen when) and the key items' believed
    timers; each member's role, goal, health, armour, weapons, mode and last words. Printed every
    few seconds and on `status` from the console, and kept as a timeline. Held by a test that a
    match plays to the same bits with the narrator on and off.
  - **M18r14d, a real match replayed and read** (lab `se-live`): the match's video following the
    hottest player is in (`client/demo_autotrack.hpp`, `-demovideo <mvd> <file> --autotrack`: by
    kit and kill streaks, cut with a margin, a dwell and a wait after a death; held by
    `tests/shooters_edge_demo_autotrack_test.cpp`; 37 cuts over a 10-minute 4on4). Open: a bot's recording re-fed to a
    fresh fighter gives back its commands bit for bit, so `ge why` answers a live match's
    decisions here, and a changed bot run over it shows where it would have decided otherwise.
    The lab reads one or two match folders with the server's own multiview recording (the truth)
    and sets each bot's beliefs against it (where each enemy was against where it was believed,
    each key item's timer against its respawn, the team lines read and missed), with the kill map
    by area, item control over time, hit rates per weapon and humanness for the bots and the
    people of the same match side by side; without a folder, "not run".
  - **M18r14e, the loop**: `se-live --matches=<dir>` sets match after match side by side, so a
    change to a bot is read in frags, deaths, items held, hit rates and humanness against the same
    people; a play session's checklist (the server, the processes, collecting the folders).
  - **M18r14f, the bots' play read off live matches**: **in progress.** Each change found by
    watching a live 4on4 (e1m2 on an existing server) and judged in `se-team --client-fed=1` over
    three seeds before and after. Landed: the map's team plan (`bots/map_plan.hpp`: areas off the
    key items by run time, their worth, ways in and height; a base, a forward area and the rushes'
    lead times); danger spots (a retreat heals away from where it or a mate died, areas called
    lost and calls for help); the mega healed at as a box and hot spots skipped; a weak bot heals
    with nobody about; a fight stood within the double's range; blasts held near mates; the dodge
    (`plugins/shooter/dodge.hpp`, the `dodge` trait: side steps across the enemy's line turned at
    drawn times, never off a ledge, into a wall or into liquid; every bot's hit rate 30 % to 27 %);
    the watch (`bots/watch.hpp` over the bot's `look`: a standing bot or a holder in its area
    watches its ways in, so a man coming in is pre-aimed at); the grenades taken up at a room's
    range; e1m2's yellow armour and launcher in reach (people take them 54 and about 60 times a
    20-minute match; the bots never had): the nav hops up stairs too high to walk and too shallow
    to run up on (a standing jump, `plugins/shooter/nav.hpp`; the pilot's `approach_jump` flies
    it), a bot just out of the water walks onto its exit node before its route, and a pocket it
    dived into for an item still there is not escaped from; the launcher now held 5 to 15 % of a
    match. People's numbers to play against: 183 real 4on4 e1m2 matches' KTX stats (20.6 frags a
    minute, the launcher 43 % of kills, 284 units/s average speed; the bots 8 to 9 in `se-team`,
    13 live, 185 units/s). The push (`bots/mind.hpp`): a strong player who believes the base
    the enemy's calls `attack ya [X]` from outside one of its ways in, his mates gather there and
    go in together (5 to 15 pushes a match; neutral on frags over six seeds). Measured against
    people with the same code (`se-people`: six real 20-minute e1m2 4on4 recordings and the bots'
    own): an enemy in plain view 25.5 % of the time alive (bots 25.8 %), in the view cone 20.7 %
    (bots 18.5 %), one within 1000 units 84 % (bots 88 %), so the bots are where people are;
    what differs is firepower: a loaded launcher 32 % of the time (bots 10 %), 3.4 rockets a
    player-minute (bots 0.55), damage given 398 a player-minute (bots 160, `se-team`'s
    `damage-per-min`). The launcher is now worth a long run to a player without it and rockets
    to one running dry, and fired as loosely as people fire it (rockets fired up 39 %, pickups
    23 %, a loaded launcher unchanged). Our recordings now carry each player's name and team.
    The opening (`bots/mind.hpp`): every player says where he spawned, and half a second later
    the two of a team with the shortest run to the quad (over the nav, from the spawns said)
    claim and rush it, the rest the armour and the launcher. Spam at news (`bots/spam.hpp`): with
    the launcher and 20 less the aggression trait in rockets, a bot fires at a way into its area
    that leads from where an enemy was seen, heard or called out lately, the view on its hands;
    rockets are stocked before a launcher is had. The maps' places named on `se-team`'s map and
    the team view (`shooter::loc_places`). Open: the bots never carry more than 7 rockets (one of
    e1m2's two rocket boxes is never reached: a nav gap), so the spam seldom has rockets to
    spare; dm3's nav reaches none of 12 items (the yellow armour, the grenade launcher, the
    lightning gun, the mega, the rocket boxes); dm2's bots stand at doors and buttons (105 units/s);
    the bots' speed (204 units/s against people's 301 by `se-people`).
    Every item a path (`se-reach`: e1m2, dm1 to dm6): the water is part of the nav (swim nodes,
    `shooter::nav_add_swim`, played by the pilot's `swim` mode); pockets no route reached are
    bridged by wider proofs (`nav_bridge_pockets`) and, last, by rocket jumps (`NavKind::rocket`,
    `prove_rocket_jump` off the mod's blast; planned only with the launcher, a rocket and 70
    health); nodes with no way back are traps no bot aims at. 246 of 249 items on foot or
    swimming, the other 3 (dm2's ledge) only by a rocket jump with no way back until trains are
    ridden. Bots keep a drifting lane off the route's line within the floor's room (`nav_room`,
    `PilotSettings::lane`), and the overviews draw the path each means to walk (`--paths=0`,
    `paths 0|1` in the bot console). Open: func_train rides (dm2); a rocket jump played live.
    The flip (`RocketJumpSpec::back`), the skilled rocket jump: the player turns into the jump
    strafing (the keys a hand presses nearest the way, pressed for the view the hands will show
    when they land), fires down behind him with the jump and strafes the view back round in the
    air. It flies 45 % farther a second than the shot at the feet with a 40 % lower apex, and its
    pitch places it, so the bridge tries it first, at five pitches from a run, half a run and
    standing, and keeps the one that lands on its target; the pitch rides on the link
    (`NavLink::pitch`) and the pilot fires that. A rocket link must start where a pilot can start
    it (the floor behind for the run and the turn, no wall in the way). `run_route` flies the
    blast without a server; the pilot test lands running and standing flips with human hands.
    dm2's ledge is now reached by a standing flip; no map's `se-team` digest moved.
    People's rocket jumps read off their recordings (`shooter/rocket_jumps.hpp`, lab `se-rj`): a
    jump is the player's own rocket (flown along his view) bursting by him and below his middle,
    not at another player, then a push away from it, rising, and a flight of 0.3 s or more; its
    kind by the view against the flight, the pitch, the run, the keys read off the move (the
    friction put back on the ground) as sequences, the landing. Players are graded amateur,
    intermediate and expert by their median speed. On 51 public dm2 games: 312 jumps, 72 % flips
    (run at 424 units/s, fired 44 degrees down, 408 across at 638 units/s) against the shot at
    the feet's 79 degrees and 200 across at 380; the back key at the shot in half of them, the
    run's keys forward, forward and a side, the side, back (the strafe into the turn); experts
    rocket jump 1.9 times in 10 minutes (84 % flips), amateurs 0.3 (69 %). 20 tricks (the same
    takeoff and landing again and again; tricks.tsv), all experts' flips. dm3 and e1m2 4on4 games
    hold few (121 and 49), with a residue of level shots read as jumps (a rocket's shooter misread
    in a crowd). Next: the bots' three rocket-jump levels from this table, and the tricks proven
    into the nav as rocket links.
    Every behaviour a switch (`plugins/shooter/features.hpp`): the bots' features (the calm view,
    the view at a hand's pace, fighting in water, the 120-degree screen, the kept target, the lead,
    the lane, the hop, short goals on the way, the commitment, danger spots, the dodge, the watch,
    the spam, packs, team talk, the map's plan, rocket and grenade jumps, air steering, surfacing)
    each on or off by name: `se-team --features=` (and `--features-red=`/`--features-blue=`, an
    A/B by team in one match), `sebots --features=`, the window's `--bot-features=`,
    and the bot console's `features -dodge,+rj`. On by default (no digest moved); a plain bot
    (`plain`) still plays: 7.9 frags a minute over six seeds of e1m2 against the full bots' 12.1,
    and a plain red team scored 289 to a full blue team's 295 over six seeds. Each switch read
    head to head (red without it, blue full, twelve seeds against the full set): the calm view
    loses about 61 frags a six-seed set, the lead about 47, the 120-degree screen wins about 36;
    the rest lie within the noise (about 35 a set). Rocket and grenade jumps never run on e1m2.
    Why the calm view lost (se-team's per-side block, `--sides=1`): with an enemy in plain view
    the side without it had him on screen 74 % of the time, the calm side 67 %; the misses lie to
    the side and behind, 42 % of them in the calm walk, whose view holds still where the view
    snapped to every turn of the route without it (164 degrees a second against people's 107).
    People look within 15 degrees of the way they move 47 % of the time on the move with nobody in
    view (se-people's `moving-view-off`; the calm bots 20 %), but a calm view along the motion
    gained nothing over twelve seeds. THE GLANCE did (`glance`, `shooter/bot.hpp`): a calm walker
    looks aside every 1.2 to 2.5 s, 75 or 120 degrees, to the side whose line of sight runs
    longest, and back at the calm view's pace: about 54 frags a six-seed set head to head (12
    seeds), the view still turning 104 degrees a second (people 107, fast turns 6.3 % against
    6.0 %); both sides glancing, frags and damage unchanged within the noise. A novice does not
    glance (his preset: tunnel vision), which keeps the temperaments apart (with it, aggressive
    and cowardly novices traded the lead in blocks of four seeds). The `trial` switch (off in every
    preset) is what the code under an A/B reads, the per-bot replacement of an environment switch.
    With the glance in, the lead and the map's plan read even head to head; the dodge still costs
    about 39 frags a set (the dodger gives less damage and is hit as often), and pressing its step
    on top of the route's side key, or not in a retreat, did not change that. Why: the aim led
    on the velocity the server sends, reading a strafer's turn the instant it came, and did not
    allow for its own strafe, so the dodger's aim paid for it. Two switches, on in every preset:
    `eye` (a change of course read a reaction late, `Contact::course` in `shooter/senses.hpp`) and
    `selfaim` (the view worked out from where its eye will be when the hands catch up). With both,
    the dodge reads even head to head, accuracy rises 31.7 -> 35.9 % (frags and damage unchanged)
    and the side with `selfaim` hits 36.3 % against 30.4. `se-people` measures the fight's feet and
    the water alike for people and bots: across an enemy's line people move 174 u/s, bots 94; people
    are waist deep 9 % of their lives, bots 37 % (`wet`; se-team `team.wet`), wading along pool
    floors at half a person's speed: the next gap. Asked of people's recordings (`se-people
    --samples`: every sample's place, wet, eyes under, speed): on e1m2 the central pool was a
    fifth of a bot's life (people 2 %), entered twice as often and kept 2.5 times as long; people
    swim it fully under too, at 176 u/s. Three faults in the pilot, fixed under the switch
    `pools`: a swimmer never planned again (stuck and lost ran only on the ground), so a bot
    knocked into a pool walked a link far above it; a pool's floor nodes were reached only
    standing on them, so a swimmer over them circled; and swimmers pressed against a channel's
    lintel. Now a floor is swum level just off it (its friction slows a swimmer), dived to only
    where the way is blocked at that height, and the pilot's wish is turned under the aim's view
    in three dimensions. Wet 36 -> 29 %, speed 162 -> 172 u/s, damage 200 -> 220 a player-minute
    (six seeds; neutral head to head). Next in the water: the fight in it (half the wet time).
    Why dry fights do not kill, measured alike (`se-people`: each weapon owned, the health and
    armour, and the weapon sounds a player-minute by sound): people fire 3.9 rockets a minute to
    the bots' 1.1, own the launcher 33 % of the time to 11 %, and fight at 98 health and 29 armour
    to 61 and 12. The launcher alone is most of the damage gap (398 against 218), so item control
    is the lever. `se-team` breaks each key item's untaken time down by what the bots made of it
    (`team.lying.<item>`: nobody believed it there, nobody wanted it, the one who did was
    fighting, one on his way or standing on it): the yellow armour lies 72 % of the match while a
    bot is on its way two thirds of that time and not arriving. On e1m2 the nav misses a stair
    tread narrower than its grid on one side of the armour's walkway, so bots from that side take
    a long jump across the pool, miss and swim round; people hop up either side's steps.
    SCRIPTED ROUTES to key items: a map's last steps to an item written as people take them
    (`bots/item_routes`, e1m2's yellow armour hopped up either stair), proven by the move
    (`shooter/nav_script`: one key rule for the proof and the pilot, the entry left only lined
    up) and added as one `script` link carrying the door over it; item rules beside them (e1m2's
    rockets over the pit never before the quad); the switch `routes`. Head to head (six seeds) the
    side with routes wins by about 40 frags, the pace unchanged; the yellow armour still lies
    76 % (bots on their way and not arriving: next). Tools: the `se-spot` lab (a place from above
    by height with the nav, items, doors, buttons and teleporters; wireframes; inside views as
    shaded, depth, slopes and normals; cuts) and se-reach's `--route` and `--floors` probes.
    HONEST EYES AND A PERSON'S HAND: sight stops at a water's surface as at a wall (a swimmer
    under it is not seen from the air), a pack is gone for only when seen, and a lost enemy is
    followed a reaction's worth then held at the corner (`corner`). The view is a hand on a mouse
    (`engine/ai/mouse.hpp`, feature `mouse`): minimum-jerk strokes timed by Fitts' law (a
    180-degree flip in about 0.22 s landing within 5 degrees for an expert, 0.32 s and 15 for an
    average player, 0.5 s and 45 for a novice), corrections planned at the eye's pace, the pad's
    reach and lifts, a right hand's drag right running down; every writer of the view (the aim,
    the calm view, jumps, swims, a button or a doorway aimed at) goes through it. A shot weapon's
    stroke is timed to land as the weapon comes ready (only the beam is traced), and fire is
    clicked, faster as health runs out. `se-people`'s `hand.*` measures people and bots alike
    (sweeps held dead level: people 3 %, the bots 60 % before and 4-6 % now; reversals a second
    0.09, 0.74 and 0.24); head to head the hand side gives up about 50 frags a six-seed set to the
    old spring's aim, which turned faster than a hand can. Off the aim, a stroke the other way
    waits until the one under way is 90 % done (`MouseWant::turn_back`), so a hop's side switch no
    longer snaps the view round; a swim's way nearly straight overhead is swum under the view the
    hand has instead of turned to. Reversals 0.27 -> 0.11 a second (people 0.09); while hopping
    (`se-people`'s `hop.*`, faster than 340 u/s across) 0.49 -> 0.24 (people 0.095). The same
    read finds people hopping 36 % of their time alive on e1m2 and the bots 8 %.
    A rocket jump's shot goes through the hand too: the pilot says the shot coming (its view and
    the time to it), the hand's stroke onto that view lands with it (the aim gives way inside the
    stroke's time), and the shot waits until the view is there. Under the hand every rocket jump
    had fired level (a rise of 44-59 units, a plain jump); on dm2 11 of 14 now rise 152-285, the
    view at the shot moving at most 17 degrees (it had snapped up to 177).
    A door only a button opens, resting shut, is no longer waited at (feature `locked`): when the
    route planned crosses one, its button is gone to round the door and pressed or shot, then the
    goal is planned again through it; a button reached only through its own door leaves that door
    off the routes a while. On dm2 the bots had stood a fifth of their time at such doors (the
    yellow armour's, the way to its teleporter): speed 106 -> 131, the yellow armour lying 88 ->
    76 %, the launcher 63 -> 46 %, rocket jumps 8.7 -> 15.5 a match; e1m2 unchanged or better.
    A floor that is a door is a floor: a plate a button slides away (dm2's big room and its
    entries, a door with no name moving with the named door it touches) stands in the nav at rest,
    its nodes shut while it is away. The room had been a hole in the nav and one spawn an island
    (a bot stood there all match). Teleporters are walked into their trigger, never cut across
    toward where they send. dm2, six seeds: frags a minute 2.1 -> 4.3, damage 129 -> 207, speed
    131 -> 173; e1m2 bit-identical. A plat raised over a bot is stepped out from under, and one
    already rising a step over its feet is not walked onto: a bot had walked in under dm2's plat
    and, standing in its field, held it up for the rest of the match (stuck 660 -> 402 a set).
    A bot fallen into lava, slime or water under a ledge it cannot climb to rockets out
    (`shooter/escape.hpp`, feature `escape`): backed against the wall under the ledge, the view
    down, the rocket fired at the pit's floor as it rises to the surface, thrown up and back onto
    the ledge. Each map's escapes are proven by the move (dm2: 35, in both lava pits), each liquid
    node linked to the cheapest, and `se-reach --escapes=fly` flies every one with the pilot (31
    of the dm2 escapes fired land). dm2's `no route` had been bots burning in its lava with no link
    out. Live, dm2's deep lava burns about 120 health a second, so a shot often comes too late: 2.5
    escapes fired a ten-minute match, 1 landed out. Head to head on dm2, red without it: +62
    against +21 (seeds 1-6), +15 against +47 (7-12): neutral; e1m2 +4 against +7 (no escape used:
    its water has ways out).
    Every death drops a pack, and the bots know it (`bots/packs.hpp`, feature `packinfer`): the
    pack placed where the victim was seen to fall, else where his last cry was heard, else (a kill
    of its own nobody saw, a rocket into the water) where its rocket's blast was heard; the weapon
    in it guessed from the shots heard from him by the player's ear: a novice believes a launcher
    whenever he heard one fired, an average player the last weapon heard, an expert knows it for
    certain inside that weapon's refire, because a weapon select waits until the weapon is ready
    again (the baseline mod now holds it so too). Scored against the server's truth (se-team
    `team.packs.guess.<skill>.right`): novice 84-86 %, average 88 %, expert 88-89 %. A pack from a
    fight with rockets in it is gone for from far; any other only by a novice (he wants
    everything) or in sight when short of ammunition. The call carries the place (`rl pack [ya]`,
    an `@ ya` read too) and is answered (`coming [ga]`), and the rest leave the pack to the one who
    answered. People take 99 % of packs, 85 % within 10 s (se-people `packs-*`), and so do the
    bots. Head to head on e1m2, red without it: +6 and +59 a six-seed set for the side with it.
    The view's looks aside are SWIPES: the glance goes out and back at the pace of a deliberate
    turn and holds 0.4 s from when it lands (enemies seen on the screen 57.7 -> 61.6 %), and a
    calm bot that hears an enemy where it knew of nobody swipes there and holds twice its
    reaction time (`listen`: +23 a six-seed set head to head; it runs about five times a minute
    across eight bots, since a player makes sound only when he jumps, fires, takes or cries).
    A hopper's stretch ends where the route climbs (`stairs`): stairs are walked and the hop
    taken up again at the top (+16 a six-seed set head to head).
    MOVING AS FAST AS PEOPLE (speed passes for a person more than frags do). People's frames
    read one by one (se-moves `--track`, twelve dm2 matches): they jump on the landing frame,
    fly past 380 with the view on the velocity (the side key alone), and make their speed ON THE
    GROUND by a circle jump: a standing jump at 400-480 comes out of a curve, forward and a side
    key, the view on the velocity, the speed rising 347 -> 424 over its last 0.3 s while the
    heading turns 51-75 degrees. The bots now do it (`circlejump`, `plugins/shooter/
    circle_jump.hpp`, held through the move by `tests/shooter_circle_jump_test.cpp`): a hop's
    first jump curves into a turn of the route until the skill's speed; on dm2 takeoffs past
    360 doubled, neutral head to head. Hops run on along lava lips (`liphop`, the brink guard on
    the feet in the air too): dm2's hop share 9-11 % -> 14-15 % (people 36 %) with no more lava
    deaths, at about 50 frags a six-seed set, kept for the speed. A hop's jump is held under a
    lintel (`headroom`) and a stair's climb is judged by the player's own feet (wall stops in
    hops 344 -> 135 in six three-minute dm2 seeds). Next for the speed: hop into teleporters,
    keep the view's scan while hopping, and hop in fights (62 % of the bots' fast walking).
  - **M18r14g, the plan**: **in progress.** Every feature planned for the bots, as a checklist
    worked down in order. Each item is built, judged headless as below, ticked here with its
    before and after, and lands in a pull request of its own. Locations an agent needs (the
    player's game folder, people's recordings, scratch output) are named by the keys of the
    git-ignored `.bots/local.md`: `basedir` (the packs), `demos` (people's recordings, one folder
    a map: `<demos>/<map>`) and `work` (traces, A/B runs); the `bots` skill says how to fill it.
    - **How a change is judged.** Two axes, always both: frags head to head, and a HUMAN measure
      against people's (se-people `hand.*`, sighting, speed, item control, wet). A feature that
      brings a human measure closer to people's is kept at a small frag cost (within a set's
      noise, about 35); it is dropped only when it is costly AND no more human, or when it moves
      a human measure away from people's. For movement, SPEED comes first: moving fast is most
      of passing for a person. HEAD TO HEAD is the verdict: one team with the feature, the other
      without, in the same 4on4 (`se-team --features-red=all,-<name> --sides=1`), against the
      same build's full-against-full set (the sides are not even), on seeds 1-6 and then 7-12 (a
      set swings about +-35). Six seeds, never one: a single match's frags swing more than most
      changes move them. Compare a variant only against a control built the same way
      (`--features=all,-x` is not a skill's preset). Every behaviour is a switch
      (`plugins/shooter/features.hpp`: `se-team --features=`, `--features-red=`,
      `--features-blue=`, the console's `features -dodge,+rj`) and lands with its default and its
      A/B by toggling it. A change that should help and hurts is read for what it set off; a
      behaviour that rarely runs cannot move a number (count how often it runs first); and when
      the bots lag people somewhere, people's recordings are asked how they play it before
      guessing. A change that does not hold up is reverted and written under Tried and dropped.
    - **The yardstick** (183 public 4on4 e1m2 team matches' statistics, and recorded matches read
      by `se-people`; the bots' column as of the last full read):

      | Measure | People | Bots |
      | --- | ---: | ---: |
      | Frags a minute, both teams | 20.6 | 12.1 |
      | Damage given a player-minute | 398 | 208 |
      | Speed, units/s | 284 | 165-185 |
      | Time hopping (`hop.share`, past 340 u/s across) | 36 % | 8-15 % |
      | An enemy faced while in plain view (120-degree screen) | 84 % | 68 % |
      | Firing while an enemy is in plain view | 37 % | 24 % |
      | The view's turn, degrees/s (past 360: share) | 107 (6 %) | 103 (7 %) |
      | Yellow armour: lying untaken, wait from showing to taken | 11 %, 3.8 s | 72-76 %, 57 s |
      | Mega health: lying, wait | 10 %, 5.3 s | 40 %, 30 s |
      | Launcher: lying, wait | 5 %, 3.1 s | 38-44 %, 24 s |
      | Accuracy: scattergun, rocket direct | 41.9 %, 15.3 % | 37-57 %, 10-11 % |
      | Packs: taken, within 10 s, wait | 99 %, 85 %, 5.4 s | 99-100 %, 86-88 %, 3.8-5.6 s |
      | Waist deep in water (`wet`); an enemy faced while wet | 9 %; 7 % | 25 %; 42 % |
      | Across an enemy's line, faced (`fight-across`) | 174 u/s | 94 u/s |
      | Rockets fired; scattergun shots, a player-minute | 3.9; 18.5 | 1.1; 10.8 |
      | Launcher owned; health; armour | 33 %; 98; 29 | 11 %; 61; 12 |
      | The hand: sweeps dead level; pitch frozen while turning | 3 %; 2.5 % | 6 %; 1.1 % |
      | The hand: reversals a second; peak/mean; lifts a min. | 0.09; 1.77; 6.3 | 0.19; 1.79; 5.1 |
      | The hand: pitch per degree right / left | -0.008 / +0.008 | -0.008 / +0.017 |
      | dm2: time at 320+ u/s; p75; p95 (experts, se-moves) | 38 %; 428; 492 | 24 %; 361; 409 |
      | dm2: flights off at 380-460 ending in a wall (one frame -40) | 27 % | 62 % |

    - **Done**:
      - Bots on live servers across an ocean: shots led on the measured round trip, the
        intercept and the grenade's lob, buttons through relays, a live feed and a control file.
      - Rocket jumps read off people's recordings (`se-rj`), tricks in the nav by level, grenade
        jumps, flights steered in the air.
      - Team strategy by team lines (`lets take quad`, `camp ra and rl`, `free play`), a leader,
        the opening by priority from any map; launchers in backpacks called and fetched.
      - A map's goals catalogued from the map (`bots/goals`); the item goal held until news.
      - The calm view, up for air under open water, fighting in water with the view on the
        enemy; a 120-degree screen of sight with every enemy on it reacted to.
      - The view at a person's pace (turn 162 -> 103 degrees a second; people 107).
      - Short goals on the way (health, armour, a missing weapon, the mega and rockets within 256
        units, never while backing off): frags a minute 10.6 -> 12.1, damage 184 -> 208.
      - Item control measured alike for people and bots (se-people `item-lying.*`,
        `item-wait.*`); the fight's feet and the water measured alike (`fight-across`, `wet`,
        `faced-wet`, se-team `team.wet`).
      - Bot features as switches: 21 behaviours and more since, on or off per bot, per team and
        from the console; a plain bot plays at 7.9 frags a minute against the full set's 12.1.
      - THE GLANCE (`glance`, on but for novices): every 1.2-2.5 s a swipe of +-75 or +-120
        degrees to the longest open line, held 0.4 s; head to head +68 and +40; made a swipe
        later (sighting 57.7 -> 61.6 %).
      - 0a, the switches head to head: the dodge cost because the aim read a strafer's turn the
        instant the server had it; `eye` (a course change read a reaction late) and `selfaim`
        (the view worked out ahead on the bot's own velocity). `eye` was later found the
        biggest single cost and is OFF in every preset (scattergun 34 -> 40 %, rockets direct on
        fast targets 15 -> 29 %, frags a minute 10.2 -> 11.8).
      - 0b, the water, part one (`pools`): three pilot faults fixed (stuck and lost while
        swimming; a pool's floor nodes reached from above; floor links swum level, dived only
        when blocked) and e1m2's water lift deadlock (a swimmer under a raised plat held it up):
        wet 36 -> 25 %, speed 163 -> 186.
      - 0c0, why dry fights do not kill: hits by the target's state (se-team `team.target.*`);
        the arms measured alike (`owned.<weapon>`, `health`, `armour`, `shots-per-min.<sound>`):
        bots fight with the scattergun at 61 health and 12 armour, people with the launcher at 98
        and 29, so ITEM CONTROL is the lever; why the key items lie (`team.lying.<item>` and its
        causes: going far 41 %, near 25 %, on it 0-2 %); e1m2's YA steps found (treads 16 deep
        the 32 grid missed; doors over the top steps opened by a shot button; `NavSettings::treads`
        kept as a switch, off).
      - 0c1's first parts, SCRIPTED ITEM ROUTES (`shooter/nav_script`, `NavKind::script`,
        feature `routes`, `bots/item_routes`: e1m2's YA up both stairs, proven at nav build, drawn
        by se-spot, tabled by se-reach): head to head routes win about 40; item rules
        (`item_rules`: e1m2's rockets over the SNG pit no goal while the quad is up or due); why
        the YA lies "going" (se-team `--trace`: 234 trips toward it, 11 arrive; the rest end by
        a fight, a retreat, a switch to health, a death; two or three bots of both teams go at
        once 55 % of the time).
      - 0f, THE HAND: no sight through water or walls (`water_opaque`, `corner`); the mouse hand
        (`engine/ai/mouse.hpp`, `mouse`: minimum-jerk strokes at Fitts' time, the eye's
        corrections, the pad's reach and lifts, the arc, tremor; turns meant at full pace, the
        calm view slower); shots timed, not traced (the stroke lands as the weapon comes ready;
        only the arc is traced); the finger (`ai::Finger`: presses 45-90 ms, gaps by stress, a
        share synced to the refire). Every writer of the view goes through the hand, each named
        (`Bot::set_by`); a way nearly overhead swum under the hand's view (reversals 0.27 ->
        0.19/s); the hopper's sweep (`MouseWant::turn_back`: hop reversals 0.49 -> 0.24/s,
        people 0.095); rocket jumps through the hand (a bug fired them level: the pilot now says
        the coming shot and holds it until the view is there; 11 of 14 rise 152-285).
      - 0f2's dm2 parts: THE DOOR'S ERRAND (`locked`: a route across a shut door goes round by its
        button; speed 106 -> 131); FLOOR DOORS AND TELEPORTERS (a named plate door resting shut is
        a floor; `NavLink::teleport` steered into its trigger: frags a minute 2.1 -> 4.3);
        plat 206's trap (a ridden mover hanging over the feet is in the way); THE ESCAPE
        (`plugins/shooter/escape.hpp`, `escape`: out of lava, slime or water by a rocket fired at
        the surface onto the nearest ledge, proven a grid column at nav build;
        `se-reach --escapes=fly`: 31 of dm2's 35 land).
      - 0f2b's first parts: THE BRINK (`plugins/shooter/brink.hpp`, `brink`: the keys into lava
        or slime let go of, a run braked, the jump let go of, on the ground and in the air;
        `NavLink::brink`, `NavGraph::harm`; a start-open bridge usable only all the way out):
        dm2 lava deaths 67 -> 5 a set, frags a minute 6.2 -> 8.4, head to head -90 without it;
        GAP JUMPS READ OFF PEOPLE'S RECORDINGS (`shooter/gap_jumps.hpp`, se-rj's gap table: dm2,
        51 matches, 8418 gap jumps, 49 tricks).
      - 0h, STAIRS ARE WALKED (`stairs`): a hop's stretch ends at a walk link climbing 10 or more,
        so the hop starts again at the top.
      - 0i, A SOUND HEARD, NOBODY IN VIEW (`listen`): a swipe toward it at a turn meant's pace,
        held twice the reaction median, swiped back (~5 a minute for eight bots).
      - 0k, EVERY KILL DROPS A PACK (`packs.hpp`, `packinfer`): what it holds inferred by skill
        from the shots heard (right 84-89 %), where it lies inferred, fetched from a rocket fight
        or by a novice; the call `rl pack [ya]` answered `coming [ga]`. Pack control was not a
        gap (people 99 %, bots already 100 %); head to head +6 and +59.
      - 1b's first parts, the hop: WHO MOVES FASTEST (se-moves `--dir`: speed at 320+ with blasts
        cut out, ranked; the weave by speed; `--track` every player's frame); the bots' weave
        already matched people's by speed: the speed never built. Wall stops 344 -> 135 (the
        `stairs` rule by the feet; `headroom`: no jump into a low ceiling over the route); hop
        speed experts 276 -> 303, averages 236 -> 281. `liphop` (on: a hop runs on along a lava
        lip, the brink holding the feet off it; hop share 8.6 -> 13.7 %, costs ~50 frags a set:
        kept, speed first). THE CIRCLE JUMP (`shooter/circle_jump.hpp`, `circlejump`: a hop's first
        jump curves into a turn of the route with forward + side and the view on the velocity, to
        430 u/s for experts and 380 for averages): takeoffs past 360 doubled, head to head
        neutral. People's frames read: chained jumps on the landing frame, the view riding the
        velocity past 380, the speed made on the ground by a circle jump, ~450 held with a ground
        frame or two between hops; people hop off 64-400 unit drops at ~440 and keep the speed.
    - **Open, in order** (the ids are the plan's own and do not run in sequence):
      - **P1, ITEMS TIMED AS PEOPLE TIME THEM** (the maintainer, 2026-11-20: "if we can make the
        bots time items properly we will make them stronger, more bold, they will meet naturally
        at these item locations when the items spawn, there will be fights, and we will raise most
        of those numbers"; FIRST IN ORDER). The read behind it (dm2, people's 2on2 recordings
        against 2on2 expert bot matches, `se-team --size=2`; 18b's 2on2 correction): a showing
        red armour taken 3.6 s after it shows by people, 61 s by the bots (the mega 7.8 / 39, the
        quad 1.1 / 24, the launcher 1.5 / 31); the red armour taken 1.93 / 0.55 a player-minute;
        the armours' areas held (YA, RA, MH) 55 / 32, 67 / 41, 58 / 19 % and contested 23-26 /
        7-9 %; enemies coming into view 20.3 / 12.6 a minute; deaths 2.9 / 1.4 a player-minute.
        As a key item shows, the bots' goals are mostly THE QUAD (se-team `shows.tsv`). THE
        WORK, in order: (a) why the quad holds them (the team's roles, the quad's due, the goal's
        worth over the time to it: the decision log's `role` and `item_goal` with its options at
        each showing); (b) every key item TIMED off what a player knows (seen or heard taken, a

        both teams then meeting there; (c) the same in 4on4 (people's e1m2 4on4 recordings).
        YARDSTICKS (people's, 2on2 then 4on4): `show.<item>.wait-p50` and `.taken-1s`, `area.<item>.
        held` and `.contested`, pickups a minute, enemies into view a minute, deaths a minute;
        frags head to head, both sides.
        (a) FOUND (the decision log, a 2on2 match): the quad's `rush` took "the two nearest the
        quad due" of a team: in a 2on2 the whole team, both waiting on the quad from 12-15 s
        before it was due (105 of 262 role choices). `rushsize` ON (half the team rushes, one at
        least; a 4on4 unchanged): 12 2on2 seeds, the mega lies 39 -> 28 s after it shows (people
        8), the red armour 61 -> 52 (3.6), the launcher 31 -> 25 (1.5), the quad 24 -> 30 (1.1);
        the areas held up 3-8 points; deaths a minute even (1.44 -> 1.46, people 2.9); head to

      - **P2, THE HAND LESS ROBOTIC** (the maintainer, 2026-11-20: "making the bots less robotic,
        that's also a priority"; SECOND IN ORDER, worked beside P1). The read (se-people `hand.*`,
        2on2, people / bots): view sweeps 62 / 107 a minute, big ones 24 / 43, the hand lifted 6.6
        / 14.3 a minute, the view's reversals 0.12 / 0.52 a second, sweeps flat in pitch 2.7 / 16
        %, the pitch held flat 1.5 / 5.5 %. A person turns the view less often, in fewer and
        smoother strokes, and lets it rest. THE WORK: the view's writers counted by who turns it
        (se-team `team.view.<writer>.*`) and the busiest calmed first (the calm walker, the
        glances, the sound swipes, the aim's re-acquisitions), each judged on `hand.*` against
        people's and head to head. Also the rest of what gives a bot away at a glance (a fixed
        list kept here as it is found: the view's flat pitch; stops and turns on the spot).
        FIRST READ (se-team `team.view.<writer>.*`, 12 2on2 seeds): the view reverses 0.23 times a
        second overall; the door errands' and whims' button view (`poke`, bots/buttons.cpp
        `button_poke_cmd`: the view set straight on the button each command it is in sight within
        900) holds it 5 % of the time at 1.44 reversals a second, a third of all of them; the hop
        starts' recipes 3 % at 2.15 (people's own recorded mouse); the air 30 % at 0.14, the aim
        25 % at 0.11. First target: the button's view through the hand, held while out of sight.
      - **0b, THE BUNNY HOP LAB AND THE HOP AS THE MAINTAINER DOES IT** (asked 2026-11-15; first
        in order). The technique, from the maintainer: the takeoff presses FORWARD AND A STRAFE
        KEY TOGETHER (forward belongs to the start only, never to the hop's air); in the air the
        mouse leans INTO the held strafe key's side; at each reversal the strafe key and the
        mouse's direction swap together and lean the other way, and so on; a TURN is the same lean
        held more toward the curve's inside, never oversteered (a sharp turn past what keeps the
        speed breaks the hop); the next jump on the landing frame. (a) A PRACTICE MAP built in the
        tree by the map compiler (`plugins/brush`, the `map-design` skill: a plan, `map build`),
        made only for hopping: a run-up straight, a long straight to weave on, gentle S-bends,
        90-degree corners at two or three radii, a U-turn, a corridor that narrows; floor and
        walls plain, no items, no harm. (b) A LAB (`se-bhop`, a drive): one bot of each level
        down the course under the keyboard and the mouse hand, reported by section: the speed in
        and out, the takeoff speed, the gain a flight, the walls met, reversals a second and the
        mouse's lean off the velocity at each, the time; pictures from above with the line
        coloured by speed; a digest for `make labs-smoke`. Stated yardsticks: people's numbers
        off the trick maps' recordings where they exist (hoppa2 is a hop map; `.bots/local.md`)
        and off dm2 (se-moves: takeoffs p50 341 / p75 401, +26..+62 u/s a flight, the view at
        each turn rate). (c) THE HOP REBUILT on the lab to the technique above (each part a
        switch, judged first on the lab, then by the usual sets and head to head), aimed at the
        speed gap measured in 0c: people gain in the air, the bots bleed; 46 % of people's
        takeoffs are at 400+, the bots' 5 %. WHERE TO START (a cloud session has no game packs
        and no recordings): the practice map and the lab need neither, built and run headless off
        the tree alone; people's yardsticks are the numbers above until a machine with the
        recordings reads hoppa2's. The hop's code: `plugins/shooter/pilot.cpp` (the hop's mode),
        `pilot_hop.cpp` (its stretch and aim), `shooter/bot.cpp` (`airview`, `airturn`: the
        view in the air), `circle_jump.hpp` (today's start); a lab beside `se-trick` and `se-hop`
        in `games/shooters_edge/labs/`.
        PART DONE (2026-11-18): (a) and (b). THE HOP COURSE (`labs/hop_course.hpp`): pieces
        (a run-up, a 2304 straight, S-bends of radius 1024, corners of 640, 320 and 176, a U-turn
        of 400, a corridor narrowing 320 -> 128), 12 219 units along its middle, turned into a plan
        and compiled in the run (`se-bhop --write-plan=<file>` for `secli map build`). THE LAB
        (`se-bhop`, a drive, ~4 s): one fighter a skill on our server with the baseline mod, sent
        to the end as an objective, every switch of its preset; read by section with people's hop
        reader. BASELINE (seed 1): expert lap 26.6 s, speed 457, takeoffs p50 431 / p75 569, 58 %
        at 400+, gain +17 a flight, landing jumps 97 %, 4 walls, no forward in the air, reversals
        1.35/s, THE MOUSE WITH THE KEY 7 % of the air (the user's hop: the view turns toward the
        held key's side all the time), lean -4.7 (the view off the velocity AWAY from the key's
        side); a crash at the 640 corner's exit (652 -> 154) and the lane after it at ~350.
        Average: lap 42.0 s, 15 walls (a weave wide into the straight's walls), the U-turn 10.5 s
        (overshot, turned back, looped: the oversteer the user names). Novice walks (no hop).
        (c) PART DONE (2026-11-18): `keyswap` ON. The lab's fault: a hop's air held NO key 29-36 %
        of the time (let go at each reversal while the view crossed the velocity, ~11 commands).
        `keyswap` holds the pilot's side key the whole flight, swapped with its side, the mouse
        hand's stroke cut at 30 % on the swap (`kSwapTurnBack`; at ease it waited for 90 %, the
        view 9 degrees past the velocity where the air adds nothing). Expert, 4 seeds: lap 25.8 ->
        21.7 s, speed 472 -> 554, walls 5 -> 1.5, no key 29 -> 2 %. dm2, 6 seeds, head to head
        +5.5 red / -2.3 blue = +1.6 a match, speed even: neutral, more human, kept. Full hop only
        (the simple hop, an average's, flies one side a flight with forward: the bare key the whole
        flight ran it into the walls). DROPPED: the key following the mouse's own turn (31.7 s).
        (c) MORE (2026-11-18): `avghop` ON: an average flies the expert's hop (the side swapped
        in the air with the mouse) instead of the simple hop. The simple hop's fault, read off the
        lab's trace (now with the hop's aim, side and air keys): its side held a whole flight, the
        hand's stroke ran the view 40 degrees past the velocity after each swap (the turn-back
        `kTurnBackAtEase` waited for 90 % of the stroke), no key carried under it, and the flight
        went straight into the wall. Average, 4 seeds: lap 35.0 -> 25.3 s, walls 9.3 -> 4.5, no
        key in the air 36 -> 3 %, the U-turn's loop gone. dm2, averages, 6 seeds: head to head
        -5.0 red / +2.2 blue = -1.4 a match, speed even (232): neutral, the maintainer's hop, kept.
        `hopsee` ON: the hop's arc checked for a wall 0.25 s ahead, not 0.12 (at 600+ units/s a
        corner's outer wall was seen too late to turn from; the aim's 0.3 s look reached into the
        corner only 0.1 s before the wall). Expert, 6 seeds: walls 2.2 -> 0.5, lap 22.8 -> 19.0 s
        (0.3 s: no walls but 3.4 reversals a second; 0.4 s thrashed at 9.9). dm2, experts, 6
        seeds: head to head -1.3 red / -3.2 blue = -2.3 a match, inside the noise; with both sides
        on it the fast hop flights (380+) meet a wall 53.9 -> 50.9 % and keep -114 -> -82 units/s,
        speed 233.9 -> 235.0: kept. THE COURSE GREW A ROOM'S SIDE EXIT (`room-exit`, the lane's
        first half: a room 768 across shifted off the middle, its exit a 128-wide mouth flush with
        one wall): dm2's fast flights in a 3-minute trace crash at such mouths (the RL room's north
        exit at y -1296 three times), half of them under the hop's own view. The course now:
        expert lap 20.5 s, walls 1.2; average 23.1 s, 1.8 (6 seeds). The lab draws the nav's route

        both arcs walled 0.25 s ahead, the clearer one taken toward the wall beside the mouth);
        then the mouths on dm2 (the RL room's north exit, y -848 at x 1640-1830).
        THE SPEED READ AGAIN (2026-11-18, after `keyswap`, `avghop`, `hopsee`; six ten-minute dm2
        matches of the presets, se-moves by one rule against people's 51): at 320+ 23.7 % of the

        maxspeed = 256), lasts 0.13 s and gains 85 u/s a second under a fight's view (`aim`, 182
        of 316) and 200 under its own (`air`), where the move gives ~900 (320 -> 440 in 0.13 s);
        a third of them sink under `hop_slow` (240) and the hop is dropped for a walk ("walking
        the route"). The course never tests it: its start is a copied recipe (449 u/s in 0.36 s).
        NEXT, FIRST: the circle jump in a match's conditions on the course (a start out of a run

        a tenth of what it may (the keys' set under the aim's view, the hand's lag on the view,
        the late keyboard), then fix it there and judge it on dm2's chain start (people 429).
        THE TURN IN THE AIR (2026-11-18, the maintainer: "the bot turns too little in the jumps;
        people turn a lot, it's in the curves you gain speed"). Measured: dm2's people turn the
        velocity 124-141 degrees a second in the air at 400-600 units/s (a flight turns 62
        degrees, p50), the bots 85 (38); the course's expert 92. `hopturn` ON (the air view led
        6 degrees into the turn, not 2) with `hoparc` ON (the side held in the air until the aim
        lies 25 degrees the other way, not 12: one wide arc a jump). Course, 6 seeds: expert lap

        0.8/1.2. dm2, 6 seeds, 10 minutes: head to head +2.7 red / +8.8 blue = +5.8 a match; the

        maintainer's swap (2026-11-18: "swap the strafe just before we land and jump, never
        earlier, not until we are heading straight; always strafe the way you turn; as speed
        grows turn less and keep it"): the side swapped only within 0.04 s of the landing once
        the heading is past the aim, the flight's turn paced to land as far past it as the last
        swap (10-35 degrees, less the faster), the view's offset set from the air move itself
        (`-asin(c / v)` for the add across the turn needs, the hand's lag on top, its tolerance
        0.5). The line now looks like the maintainer's (a takeoff where it crosses the route, one
        arc a flight), but the course loses: expert lap 19.8 -> 23.5 s, average 20.9 -> 27.6,
        walls 0.8/1.2 -> 1.7/4.5, speed 624 -> 544 (paced slow at speed it stops gaining; the
        corners and the room's side exit want a swap the landing has not reached). Tried, worse:
        the lead off a fixed rate table (lap 29.6), floors 0/2 on the lead (walls 6-12), arcs of
        15/20/25-45 degrees or shrinking only by 800 units/s, the hand's lag 0.03/0.08 s.
        `hoppitch` (the air view pitched 0.3 of the velocity's pitch, at most 8: up on the jump,
        down on the fall): even on the course (the air move flattens the wish), a look only;
        judged on dm2 next. PEOPLE'S SWAPS IN A FLIGHT (the turn's direction reversed in the air,
        hop-length flights 0.35-0.8 s off 350+): dm2's people hold one side the whole flight 48 %,
        swap once 39 %, twice 10 %, more 3 %; ztricks' (30 flights) 17 / 63 / 20 %, the swap in
        the flight's middle three fifths (26 / 39 / 32 %), almost never its last (3 %). The bots
        now: 42 / 30 / 13 / 14 % (3+ swaps: people 3 %). So the swap at the landing alone
        (`hopland`) is half of people's flights; the other half swap once mid-air.
        PEOPLE'S RHYTHM, `hoprhythm` ON (2026-11-18): the side key's swaps in a flight split by
        cause (se-team's trace has `hswap`, pilot.hpp `side_why`): dm2, own-view hop flights, 2.48

        forth every command: wall then wall 1657, wall then the aim's swing 880, swing then swing
        527 within 0.2 s), and 602 first swaps a wall's within 0.05 s of the takeoff (the side
        chosen on the ground blind to the wall). Built: a swap in the air kept 0.1 s before any
        cause swaps back; straight on, the side held, when it is clearer than both arcs (the view
        at `-asin(30 / v)`); the wall's check on the landing too. dm2 trace: 2.48 -> 1.58 swaps a
        flight, own-view flights with 3+ turn reversals 22 -> 15 %. Off the recordings (se-moves,
        flips by people's rule, 12 x 10 min): 0 / 1 / 2 / 3+ reversals 43 / 29 / 13 / 15 % ->
        45 / 29 / 13 / 13 % (people 48 / 39 / 10 / 3). Head to head, 12 seeds: +6.1 red / -4.3
        blue = +0.9 a match; speed even (320+ 23.6 -> 23.5 %, chain start 319); the course even
        (expert lap 19.8 -> 19.6 s, walls 0.8 -> 0.7). TRIED, worse: one swap a flight held to
        the landing (course walls 0.8 -> 4.8, lap 27.5 s), a wall's swap only for an arc 0.03-0.1 s
        clearer (average's walls 1.2 -> 2.7-3.5), the keep at 0.2-0.3 s (walls 1.3-3.0). What is
        left: 735 of 908 reversals still follow a key swap, now the aim's (its 25-degree hold
        crossed, or 45 off at once): the arc overshoots the aim and comes back. TRIED, on the
        course (6 seeds, against `hoprhythm`'s 19.6 s lap, 17.6 gain a flight, 136 degrees a
        second in the air, 1 % of flights on one side): THE TURN PACED to land 0-20 degrees past
        the aim when the turn now would carry it further (`hopland`'s pacing, its ban on mid-air
        swaps left out; the lead only ever lowered): the rhythm is people's (10 past: 51 % of
        flights on one side, people 48) but the air turns 80 degrees a second, the gain a flight
        falls to 9.5-12.7 and the lap to 21-23 s. THE SWAP WITH THE JUMP (`hop_hold` taken in the
        last 0.04-0.08 s before the landing, the air's hold widened to 35-90 degrees): the air
        turns faster (154-202) but the wider arcs meet walls (1.3-13.7 a lap, lap 22.5-46 s) and
        the wall's swaps keep the flights on one side at 0-8 %. Both dropped (code removed). So
        a turn at people's rate held a whole flight needs a weave wider than the course's lanes
        or the bot's aim allow. PEOPLE'S WEAVE READ (2026-11-18, expert bots, six 5-minute dm2
        seeds, against people's top third of 17 players by time at 320+; the way at a moment is
        the chord of the path +-0.4 s, flights 0.35-0.8 s taking off at 400-600, se-moves tracks
        for both): THE WEAVE IS ALREADY PEOPLE'S. Swing about the way a flight (max-min) p50 46
        degrees (people 42); heading off the way at the takeoff, signed by the flight's turn, p50
        -18 (people -13), at the landing +4 (+6); a mid-air turn reversal falls 19 degrees past
        the way (people 21; p90 48 against 41); in-flight reversals 0/1/2/3+ 27/46/20/7 %
        (33/47/15/5). Nothing to build there. THE GAP IS HOW OFTEN THE BOTS ARE FAST: flights at
        400-600 0.6 a bot-minute against people's 3.1; time moving at 400+ (the speed from the
        positions over +-0.05 s, no blast: people's per-frame speed is noisy, a mid-air frame's
        change p50 8.3 where the move allows 1.1, so single samples flatter them) people 29 %,
        22 % of it in the air, the bots 4 % and 2 %. A climb from under 300 to over 400 is the
        same for both (on the ground, p50 0.22 s, ~100 u/s of the ground's gain): people make

        Ground runs between two flights: people land at 335-341 and leave at 369-379 after
        0.2-1.0 s turning 71-137 degrees (the ground holds 370+ only turning ~230 degrees a
        second); the bots land at ~225 and leave at ~310. THE CIRCLE JUMP IN MATCHES (14 a
        bot-minute, se-team's new trace column `hend`: why the hop last ended, end, slow, room,
        restretch, plan, off): ~47 % end in a jump (takeoff p50 324-330, the run's peak 357),
