        curve's arc looked along each command, `arc_clear` 0.25 s; walled: the jump now): the
        circle's slow ends 552 -> 258 in three seeds, wall bumps 16.9 -> 16.1 a bot-minute, but
        400+ 4.4 -> 4.4 %, speed 241 -> 240; `groundcorner` (a hop's landing with its aim 45+
        degrees off the velocity curved round on the ground with the circle's keys, up to
        0.6 s): its curves end in 0.1 s turning 12-16 degrees (under a fight's view 60 % of
        them), 400+ 4.4 -> 4.3 %, ground runs leave at ~305 as before. WHY A 400+ STRETCH ENDS
        (the trace, 888 stretches in three seeds, a blast's excluded; `hend` now also names what
        a stretch ended at, `end/climb`, `room/goal`...): on the ground walking 32 % (the hop
        given up before: the stretch's end, mostly a CLIMB walked by `stairs` (~100 of ~330),
        then a mover, the goal; a new plan), on the ground still hopping 28 % (a circle's curve
        or a copied start's run at 400+, a gap's lip run on, a low ceiling), a wall in the air
        17 %, the air's own bleed 9 %. TRIED, neutral, dropped: `fastjump` (a hop on the ground
        at 390+ jumps on the landing, no curve or start's run): 400+ 3.8 -> 3.3 %, in the air
        2.4 -> 2.2 %, speed 241 -> 242. THE VIEW OFF THE VELOCITY IN A FAST FLIGHT (320-500 at
        takeoff, no blast, the view's mean offset over the flight, the gain landing - takeoff,
        both off recordings): people keep it within 20 degrees in 61 % of their flights (gain p50
        +7), the bots 46 % (-5); 20-90 degrees off people lose 36-113 a flight, the bots 166-204
        (38 % of their flights). Not the keys: in such air the bots press against the velocity
        1-6 % of commands; under the aim's view 89 % press NOTHING (`aimhop` under `hopside` may
        use the side keys alone, which lie along the velocity there), so the flight runs straight
        on into a wall. TRIED, worse, dropped: `fightkeys` (every key set allowed in a hop's air
        under the aim): speed 241 -> 235, 400+ 3.8 -> 3.7 %, the view-off bands lose as before.
        HOW PEOPLE'S VIEW LEAVES A FAST FLIGHT (its 20-90 degree flights, the offset by fifth
        of the flight, p50): 15 / 27 / 38 / 49 / 68 degrees: ON THE VELOCITY AT THE TAKEOFF and
        turned away to aim through the flight (54 % "on at the takeoff, off at the landing", 30 %
        off throughout); the bots 28 / 30 / 38 / 45 / 56 (35 % on at the takeoff, 14 % on in the
        middle only); the view turns ~180-190 degrees a second in such flights for both. NEXT:
        THE TAKEOFF LOOKED ALONG: in a fight, a hop's jump taken with the view on the velocity
        (the takeoff's command and ~0.1 s after it, the hand's own stroke), the aim's view
        given back after it; judged on the view-off share at the takeoff (people 15 degrees),
        the 400+ time, the gain of a fight's flight and frags head to head. The climbs walked
        after it (does a person keep 400 up a stair?).
        STRAFE THE WAY THE AIM TURNS, `aimstrafe` ON (2026-11-18, the maintainer: "strafe in the
        aiming direction, never turn against the strafe; aiming right then needing to aim left,
        strafe left, or stop moving a bit and resume once the rocket is off"). Measured first
        (expert bots, six 5-minute dm2 seeds; people off their recordings, air at 320+, rates
        over +-2 samples; a key's work read as the velocity's curve, so people and bots alike):
        people's velocity curves WITH the view's turn in 72 % of the samples where the view turns
        (40+ degrees a second), against it 5 %, not at all 23 %; the bots under the aim 16 %, 4 %,
        79 %: in a hop's air under the aim they pressed no key 64 % of the time (the air's set
        scored by the speed it keeps along the route, which the aim's view rarely allows), and
        the fight's dodge stepped across against the turn. BUILT (`plugins/shooter/bot.cpp`
        `aim_strafe_set`): under the aim in a hop's air the side key of the view's turn, swapped
        as the aim swings back past 0.2 degrees a command (a still aim keeps the side; the
        finger's own lag before a swap is the keyboard's), let go only while the key's wish lies
        30+ degrees past square to the velocity (the view far ahead of it: a hard brake) or while
        the flight with it held (flown 0.4 s, the velocity curving) meets a wall 0.1 s sooner
        than with none; the dodge stays off in a hop's air (`footwork.cpp`). RESULT (12 seeds pooled, two sets of
        six): no key under the aim 64 -> 26 %, the velocity curving with the turn 7.9 -> 13.2 %
        of the aim's samples (turning: 17 -> 27 %), against 2.1 -> 3.0 %; fast hop flights
        103 -> 112 a set, speed 239.7 -> 243.1, 400+ 3.95 -> 4.1 %; the flights mostly under the
        aim keep what they kept (p50 -55 -> -54, walls 38 -> 40 %; a set of six swings the fast
        flights' p50 gain -42 to -82 on one build: never judge it off one set). Head to head (12 x 10-minute dm2): +0.4 on red, +1.6 on blue,
        +1.0 a match. TRIED, dropped (code removed): `aimstill`, the other way (the aim swinging
        back with a shot due in 0.25 s lets the keys go until the shot is out, 0.5 s at most):
        fast flights 116 -> 98, speed 244 -> 241, the curve with the turn 13.2 -> 10.6 %; head to
        head +6.3 / -2.8 (+1.75, a set's noise): slower and less like people. STILL OPEN: the curve with the turn is 27 % of the bots' turning samples
        against people's 72 %: the side key is dead (the view behind the velocity on the turn's
        side) in a third of them, so the view, not the key, is the rest (THE TAKEOFF LOOKED ALONG
        above); and the dodge still steps against a circle jump's curve or a hop start's run on
        the ground under the aim (~8,600 of ~33,000 such commands in two seeds). TRIED, neutral,
        dropped (code removed): `hopkeys` (no dodge at all while the pilot hops, its run-ups
        too): 12 seeds, fast flights 112 -> 103 a set, speed 243.1 -> 242.6, 400+ 4.1 -> 3.75 %.
        TRIED, worse, dropped (code removed): `aimalong` (THE TAKEOFF LOOKED ALONG, first cut: in a
        fight, the pilot's hop view wanted from 0.12 s before a landing, by the level fall, to 0.1 s
        after the takeoff, not with a shot in sight due within 0.15 s): 12 seeds, fast flights
        112 -> 99 a set, speed 243.1 -> 240.1, 400+ 4.1 -> 3.75 %, frags a minute 13.4 -> 12.0,
        accuracy 34.1 -> 32.7 %; the view at the takeoff no nearer the velocity (p50 27-33
        degrees off, before 29; people 15) while it turned faster (215 degrees a second, before
        173; people 180-190): the hand cannot reach the velocity in a window that short and
        back, so the swing costs the aim and buys no takeoff. A longer window or a want only
        part way to the velocity is the same swing: what is left of the item is the WHY of
        people's 15 degrees (do they choose takeoffs when the aim already lies along the
        velocity? split their fight flights by the target's bearing off the velocity at takeoff).
        SPLIT BY THE ENEMY'S BEARING (fast flights, 320-500 at takeoff; the enemy the view is on
        0.15 s after the takeoff, within 25 degrees and 1500 units; his bearing off the velocity
        at the takeoff; people's track and 12 bot seeds alike, `bearing.py`): an enemy under the
        view in 26 % of people's fast flights against 49 % of the bots' (people at speed mostly
        look along, not at someone); the enemy within 20 degrees of the velocity 41 % (bots 35),
        20-45 28 % (24), 45-90 17 % (19), behind 13 % (22): people hop at their enemy more and
        away from him less. With him 20-45 degrees off, people's view at the takeoff lies 8
        degrees off the velocity, the bots' 20 (on him); 45-90 off, 44 against 57. TRIED, no
        effect, dropped: `aimalong` v2 (the aim within 45 degrees of the velocity pulled 70 % onto
        it in the same window): the takeoff's view with him 20-45 off still 19-20 degrees (the
        hand's stroke does not land in 0.2 s), flights under the aim walled 40 -> 43 %, speed
        243.1 -> 241.9. NEXT for the view: not a window but WHERE the fight's hop goes (toward
        the enemy, people's 41 %) and HOW OFTEN the fast hop aims at all (people 26 %): a fast
        flight not under the aim unless a shot is due, the aim's hold given up at speed.
        THE AIM AT SPEED WAS A TEAM SIZE (2026-11-19). TRIED, dropped (code removed):
        `fastway` (at a hop's takeoff at 320+ with the enemy 45+ degrees off the velocity, no aim
        until the landing): the view is ON him at such a takeoff, so it swung 100-160 degrees back
        to the way and landed late in the flight; the aim share 50 -> 46 %, head to head -4.0 red /
        -12.0 blue = -8.0 a match. v2, no new turn onto him (an aim already on him kept): 50 ->
        49 %, +2.3 / -0.4 = +1.0, nothing. Then the cause: people's dm2 recordings are 2on2 (3-4
        players a demo), the bots' sets 4on4. By the NEAREST enemy within 1500 (`py/aimwhen.py`),
        the bots' fast flights lie toward him and away as people's do (0-20 degrees 14 vs 13 %,
        45-90 27 vs 29, behind 43 vs 42), so the hop's direction is no gap; played as a 2on2
        (se-team's new `--size=2`, 12 x 5-minute seeds) the bots aim in 30 % of fast flights
        (people 29 %; the enemy 45-90 off 24 vs 24 %, behind 18 vs 13 %) and their distance to the
        nearest enemy is people's (within 300 18 vs 16 % of the time). The view in a fast flight is
        people's: nothing to build. Compare people and bots in the same team size from here on.
        CLIMBS AT SPEED (`py/stairs.py`: two ground samples within 0.8 s rising 40+ units, the

        WALKING FASTER, `walkmash` ON (2026-11-19, the maintainer: "when the bot reverts to walking

        2on2): people are at 320+ 57.6 % of the time, the bots 28.0 % (400+ 21.6 against 2.6 %);
        people's walks (ground stretches 0.4 s+, `py/walks.py`) run p50 322, 340+ 33 %, 360+ 17 %,
        the bots' p50 239, 14 % and 3 %: no plain run passes 320. The move (the ground's
        acceleration only tops the speed ALONG the wish up to 320: a wish 45 degrees off the run
        lets it build past, swapping sides keeps the run from turning onto the wish): steady speed
        by the hold of each side, 77 commands a second, 0.026 s 426, 0.05 s 419, 0.1 s 396, 0.125
        s 386, 0.2 s 366, forward alone 320. BUILT (`pilot.cpp` walk, `bots/keyboard.cpp`): a walk
        at a full run on dry ground, nothing foreseen to brake for, no lava or slime within 192,
        offers W and a side key swapped every 0.1 s (`curve_keys`), pressed whole while the view lies
        within 25 degrees of the run (turned under a view off it the wish was a strafe key alone:
        slower); a mashed run holds 350-420. 2on2, 12 x 5 min, both sides on: speed 240.7 -> 248.9,
        320+ 28.0 -> 30.7 %, walks p50 239 -> 264, fast hop flights 342 -> 365, frags a minute 3.07
        -> 4.12, accuracy 31.7 -> 33.1 %. Head to head (12 x 10 min, 4on4): +8.8 red / -1.4 blue =

        moves p50 285 (the route's corners, a view off the run: strafe-alone keys as often as W+A;
        the walking view within 15 degrees of the run 27 % of ground time, people 35 %, people

        A JUMP NEVER MADE IS GIVEN UP, `giveup` ON (2026-11-19). The bots' slow time split by cause
        (`py/slowwhy.py`, the trace, 2on2): 8 % of the time alive was `run_up`, 4.8 % at one spot (x
        2176-2304, y -1664, dm2): a flip rocket jump toward the quad lined up for, run at, overrun
        and lined up again every ~1.4 s for 40-90 s on end, the jump key never pressed. The give-up
        (`kRunUpTries`, 4 run-ups: missed twice, planned round) never fired: each plan again started
        the same flip from the node beside, which reset the count; and it cut only plain jumps'
        kin, which `nav_route` honours only under `jumpcuts`. BUILT: the run-ups counted by the
        jump's end (72 units) and kind; a jump given up takes its kin of its own kind (starts within
        128, ends within 72) and the cuts are honoured on every link. 2on2, 12 seeds: run-up
        commands 67 106 -> 39 606, 320+ 30.7 -> 31.4 %, walks p50 264 -> 281, frags a minute 4.12
        -> 4.70; the loop now ends after two give-ups (~11 s). Head to head (12 x 10 min, 4on4):
        +4.3 red / -1.4 blue = +1.5 a match.

        TOO TIGHT FOR THE MOVE? (the maintainer: judge the speed and the turn against the move's

        flight against the tightest circle the air turns the run on at that speed (a radius of
        about v * v * dt / 30: 88 units at 450, 156 at 600); `tight` = the radius allowed over the
        radius needed, past 1 too tight. In the decision log as the `turn` decision (`make bot-why
        ARGS="--name=turn ..."`: ok / too-tight with speed, min_radius, tight) and in se-team's
        trace as `tight`. READ (4on4, 6 seeds, `py/tight.py`): 1.6 % of hop air commands are too
        tight; own-view fast flights with a too-tight moment meet walls 30 %, those without 29-40 %
        (the straightest, worst need under half the limit, 40 %). So the bots do NOT turn tighter
        than the move allows: the walls come with a way ahead that the air could follow. At the
        hit (the velocity's change in the one command): no grazes, 49 % head-on (-298), 51 %
        oblique (-141); 77 % with the wall on the OUTSIDE of the turn the side key is making (the
        flight already curving away from it, too late or too little). NEXT: where the flight's
        line meets the wall against the route's line (a picture: the flight, the way, the wall),
        and whether the side was chosen late (the wall check's horizon at the weave's outer swing).
        LATE, NOT TIGHT: at an outside-wall hit (own-view fast flights, 319 in 6 seeds) the side key
        had swapped away from that wall p50 0.13 s before (54 % within 0.15 s), the swap's cause
        the wall check itself (`wall` 165, `wall-jump` 72, the aim's swing 54): the wall is seen
        and turned from, but at 450 the air turns ~40 degrees in 0.13 s and the keys and the view
        land a command or more after the call. A longer look (`hopseefar`) did not move it: what
        delays the call when the wall is first in the look (both arcs walled? the 0.1 s keep of
        `hoprhythm`?) is the next read, off the `turn` and `hswap` records before each such hit.
        THE PINCH (se-team's trace now records the hop's wall check, record only: `arcm`, `arco`, how
        long the held and the other side's arcs stay clear): before 97 % of the outside-wall hits
        BOTH arcs were walled within the 0.25 s look, the wall first in the look p50 0.13 s before
        the hit, and in 204 of 306 no swap followed (neither side clearer). 63 % of own-view fast
        flights meet such a pinch, p25 0.01 s / p50 0.11 s into the flight (so mostly from the
        takeoff), and those meet a wall 39 % of the time, the rest 18 %. The flight is launched
        into a space narrower than its weave at that speed; the air cannot get it out. `landwall`
        (the same check at the landing over 0.15 s, run on instead) screened worse today (251.8
        against 254.8). Next: where pinched takeoffs are (the map's cells) and how people fly
        those cells (their weave narrower? slower? not jumping there?).

        timing only when the enemy carries a droppable key item (an RL) or holds a key area (RL,

        teammates and attack together. The bots spend 40 % of their time in dm2's hub (people
        20 %), aimed at someone on the ground 40 % of it (people 24 %).

        HOLDING BY THE ITEM (the maintainer's definition, se-people `people.area.*` and its table
        by spot, `labs/areas_read.hpp`, replacing the keeper-by-presence read): a team HOLDS an
        area once it takes the key item twice in a row, an enemy walking in changing nothing; a
        holder killed within 400 of the item makes it CONTESTED until one team takes it twice in a
        row (the enemy then holds it), or, once an enemy has been killed there too, until a holder
        takes it once; the enemy taking it twice in a row while it is held makes it theirs.

        MOVING PLATFORMS BUILT: the arena rules spawned no `func_train` (and no `path_corner`):
        on our server dm2's three moving platforms never existed (the train was the campaign's
        alone); now in the common rules (common_map.cpp), the arena's path corners kept as

        walks across its top, a ride from each spot to the same spot at every other stop (its
        run and waits). The pilot (pilot_movers.cpp, pilot_route.cpp, pilot.cpp): onto it once
        its top covers the spot stepped to, off it once it stands at the stop, a ride held on
        the same spot of its top, done aboard at the ride's stop. se-reach: dm2's 30 items all
        reached on foot (3 before by rocket only). Four 20-minute matches: the high mega held 42 %
        (never before; people 70), lava deaths 7 -> 5, waits 229 -> 298, speed and frags even.
        THE WAY INTO THE SOUTH COMPLEX (the maintainer: the button near the lava opens a floor):
        button 145 runs out the two plates over the lava (t9). Our rules linked doors where they
        stood 0.1 s in, after a door that starts open had moved, so only the named plate came
        out; people walk onto the far plate from the button's side (46 times against 8 from the
        other), so doors now link by where the map drew them (common_map.cpp `boxes_touch`), and
        the nav names a plate by its group. `bridgebutton` (fighter_doors.cpp): a bridge a button
        runs out stays a way; a route across it while it is in sends the player to the button
        first (near: its run within 8 s, and the bridge reached from it within the bridge's
        time out), the bridge off the way there; a short bridge's margin a third of its wait

        a left hand whose fingers rest on a home placement: at most FIVE keys held at once, and
        five only in the best case (the move keys, jump and one more); a key out of the fingers'
        reach (a weapon key past 4, a far bind) means the hand leaves its place for a moment, its
        move keys let go or held badly while it does. A key changes in a finger's time (a press,
        a release, a roll from A to D), never in a command, and the hand misses, holds a key
        too long, and lets a forward go to switch weapons. What the pilot and the fighter want
        (forward, side, jump, the weapon) goes through these hands, as the mouse hand turns the

        person's is kept).

      - **0r, MOVEMENT RECIPES COPIED OFF PEOPLE'S RECORDINGS** (asked 2026-11-12). Every hard
        move so far was rebuilt by hand from what people do (the circle jump, the gap curve, the
        run-up, the stair hops) and each took rounds to get right; the recordings hold the moves
        themselves. Copy them: a RECIPE is a short stretch of a person's movement kept as what a
        bot can play again, entered from a ZONE where the bot settles first. The pieces:
        (1) THE READER: every track of a folder of `.mvd`s (client/demo_scan.hpp, as se-rj) cut
        into moves of known kinds: a HOP START (from a stand or a walk to the third jump of a chain
        at 400+: the start people use after a spawn, an item, a landing), a GAP or SPEED JUMP (the
        curve in and the takeoff), a ROCKET JUMP (the run, the turn, the shot and the flight), a
        STAIR or LEDGE HOP, a TELEPORTER ENTRY; each instance as frames from its entry: the feet
        and the velocity, the view (yaw relative to the entry's heading, pitch), the keys read off
        the move (the key set by the wish the acceleration shows, as `rocket_jumps.hpp` reads them
        at the shot), the jump and fire presses.
        (2) THE GATHERING: instances of one kind whose entries (feet, heading) and exits lie near
        each other gathered as tricks are (`--cell`); one made `--gold` times or more by two players
        or more is a recipe, by level (the lowest whose players made a fifth of it). Kept: its
        ENTRY ZONE (the disc its entries' feet lie in, the heading band, the speed band and whether
        they stood), its exit, its time, and MANY real instances (never the mean: a mean of
        people's curves is a curve nobody ran), kept to SPREAD over the zone: different starting
        places, headings, speeds and run-ups into the same trick (a hop start from a stand and one
        from a walk at an angle; a rocket jump from a run and one from a stand), thinned only
        where two lie close in every respect. Each instance keeps the entry state it starts from
        (feet, velocity, heading, on the ground or not) and what its player carried there (the
        weapon in hand, the launcher and its rockets, health and armour: a rocket jump's cost).
        Each recipe also records its REQUIREMENTS and its COST, worked out at the proof: what it
        needs (the launcher or the grenade launcher and the shots it fires, health and armour
        enough to come out of the blast above a floor, a quad not in hand: a quad's blast kills
        the jumper) and what it takes (the blast's health, after armour by its kind, the rockets,
        the seconds), against what it BUYS: the time it saves over the cheapest FREE way between
        its entry and its exit (the nav's best route with no blast: a walk, a gap jump, a swim).
        A TARGET AND A START, BY NAME: each recipe records where it goes and where it comes from
        in the map's own words: its TARGET the item or place its exit stands at or next to (the
        items' names the bots already call, `rl`, `quad`, `ya`, `mh`, and the team plan's areas),
        its START the area its zone lies in (dm2: the RL's ledge reached from BELOW, from the floor
        under it, and from the QUAD, by the quad's room). A bot or its goal asks by target:
        `recipes_to(recipes, "rl")` lists every way up to the rocket launcher with each one's start
        and cost, and `recipe_for(recipes, "rl", state)` the nearest that fits it now, so a goal
        that wants the RL can say "a trick up to the RL from here, if there is one" without a
        route ever having passed by. The route planner still sees each recipe as a link (its target
        the node at its exit); the names are what the goals, the team plan, the talk ("rl: going
        up") and the decision log read. A trick read off recordings whose exit is near no item or
        area is named by its place and still kept.
        GRADED, NOT ONLY THE GOOD ONES: every instance read keeps its grade and its player's level,
        and FAILED attempts are read too (a gap jump that fell short into the water, a hop start
        that stalled under 400, a rocket jump that bumped the ledge's lip and dropped back): the
        grade by how it came out (made or failed, its time against the trick's best, the speed it
        carried out, the corrections on the way). Each skill draws from its own people's: an
        EXPERT the clean ones; an INTERMEDIATE also the imperfect (slower, a lower takeoff, a
        late correction, a landing short of the line); a NOVICE those and the FAILED ones, at the
        rate people of that level fail it (a novice's gap jump fails as often as novices' do). A
        failure's proof is that the move does what it did (falls short where it fell short), and
        a failure into lava or slime is kept only at people's rate and never for a trick the
        bot's level does not try. The recipe's cost and what it buys are read for each level from
        its own instances (a novice's rocket jump saves less and fails more, so it is worth it
        less often). Data per map as the tricks are (`bots/recipes.cpp`, rows
        copied from the lab's `recipes.tsv`, quantized: a few hundred bytes a recipe); people's
        names never kept.
        (3) THE PROOF: each instance flown by the move from points across its zone
        (`prove_script`'s way, `player_move` over the map's solids), under the keyboard and the
        mouse's hands; one that does not reach its exit is left out; a recipe left with none is
        dropped and tabled. A proven recipe is one nav link (`NavKind::recipe`) from the node in its
        zone to the node at its exit, priced by its proven time, taken by bots of its level.
        (4) THE CHOICE: one call, `recipe_for(map's recipes, kind or trick, the bot's state)`,
        answers "is there a move from here, and which": the instances near the bot's feet scored
        by how far its state is from each one's entry (place, heading, speed and acceleration,
        ground or air) and barred by what it carries (no launcher or rockets, health under the
        blast's cost plus a margin, a weapon switch it has no time for), the nearest returned with
        how far off it is, or none. With many instances spread over the zone the nearest one
        usually starts close to where the bot already is, so it needs little or no lining up: the
        recipes fit the bot, not the bot the recipe. Ties are drawn off the bot's own stream, so
        two plays from one spot differ as a person's do.
        NEVER A COSTLY MOVE NEEDLESSLY: a recipe with a cost is planned only when what it buys is
        worth it to the bot now. In the route its link is priced by its seconds PLUS its health
        and rockets in seconds, at a rate set by the bot's state (health dear when low, dearer
        still with no armour; rockets dear when few) and the goal's urgency (a quad or a red armour
        about to come up, a mate calling for help, a chase, a flight: urgent; an errand or a wander:
        not); so a rocket jump over a pool a gap jump or a short walk round also crosses is never
        taken for an errand, and one onto the quad's ledge 15 s faster is taken as the quad comes
        up. A move that would leave the bot under its health floor is barred whatever it buys.
        `recipe_for` returns the cost with the instance; the decision log records the price and
        what it bought (`ge why`: why it jumped, or why not).
        (5) THE PLAY: the route leads to the zone and asks `recipe_for` as it arrives (and while
        it runs on: a better fit may come up a few frames later); only when the nearest is too far
        off does the bot SETTLE (slow or turn into that instance's entry, or stand: as a person
        lines up before a trick), then COMMITS: the instance's keys pressed through its keyboard (each key asked the hand's press
        delay early) and its view through the mouse's hand, CLOSED LOOP: at each frame the feet and
        velocity against the instance's; a small error taken out by the view (people steer a hop
        with the mouse), a big one before the commit point (the takeoff, the shot) ABORTS to the
        nav (planned again from where it is); after it, flown to the exit as a flight is.
        (6) THE STAGE: `se-reach --recipes=fly` plays every recipe of a map from points across its
        zone on three seeds in seconds (the instance chosen and how far off, settled or entered at
        once, committed, aborted, reached, time against people's), with
        `--recipe-trace` as `--gap-trace`; a recipe judged staged before any match.
        Homes: the recipe's form, its reader off a track, its proof, `recipe_for` and its play are
        any classic mover's (`plugins/shooter/recipes.hpp`); the maps' recipes and the zone's settling in the
        fighter are the game's (`games/shooters_edge/bots`); the reader's lab beside se-rj
        (`se-recipes`, or `se-rj --recipes`). Switch `recipes` (one, every kind; a kind off by
        `--recipe-kinds`). ORDER: hop starts first (speed is the Turing test: people are at 400+
        within ~1 s of a stand; the bots' circle jump takes off at ~345 and hops at ~270), on e1m2
        and dm2; then dm2's ways up to the RL (from below and from the quad: the first targets
        asked by name); then the gap curves (where one is proven it replaces `gap_run`'s rollout); then
        rocket jumps (the run-up and the turn people use, not only the takeoff and landing); then
        stairs and teleporters. Yardstick for the choice: the share of plays entered with no settling,
        and the settling time when there is some; for the cost: the health spent on jumps a
        bot-minute and what each saved (seconds, and whether its goal was urgent), against
        people's rocket jumps by situation (se-rj: health before and after, what they went for);
        no blast spent where a free way was within a few seconds. By level: each level's made share, time
        and failures a trick against the same level of people's (a novice no cleaner than a
        novice). A skill section (bots: § Copying movement off recordings) says how
        a map's recipes are read, proven and added. Yardstick: staged reached share and time
        against people's; in matches speed (people's p50 against the bots'), the time from a stand
        to 400, hop chains (se-moves `jumps.chain-3plus-share`), gap and rocket jumps made by
        level, and head to head; the movement's human measures (se-people `hand.*`, `hop.*`)
        nearer people's, since they are people's.

        5.7, speed even (232 -> 231). A tighter turn where a corridor has no room for the 95-degree
        one (135 degrees, 24 units) moved nothing (14.9) and was not kept. A one-side variant whose
        features change nothing (`--features-red=+uturn` with it on) plays bit-identical matches to
        `full`: the both-sides frag drop seen in one-side sets is no artifact of se-team.
        THE RUN-UP DANCE: 3.4 run-ups a bot-minute, 1.8 of them a RETRY within 4 s and 96 units of
        the last: lined up behind a jump, run at it, off the line at the start (the keyboard does
        not hold the run within 10 units: 15-30 off), lined up again, up to 4 times
        (`kRunUpTries`); 2.3 s of every bot-minute spent so, single dances up to 11 s at dm2's
        jumps to the RA and the YA, and only 1 in 14 ending in the jump.
        JUMPS FLOWN, NOT DANCED AT (`jumpfly`, ON in every preset; pilot.cpp `approach_jump`, `jump_flies`):
        a plain jump's run that nears its start (from 24 units behind) off the line or the speed
        jumps anyway when the jump, flown by the move from where it stands (and a command or two
        late under a keyboard), lands by the far end (a gap's landing: 48 across, 32 up or down);
        it lines up again only when none does. Expert bots, dm2, 12 five-minute seeds, without ->
        with: run-ups a match 147 -> 86, jumps 65 -> 146 (131 flown), dancing 2.3 -> 1.2 s a
        bot-minute (retries 1.8 -> 0.9; what is left is mostly the rocket jumps', not flown), stops
        12.9 -> 11.4 a bot-minute (people's experts 12.3), brief 9.3 -> 8.1 (7.6), still 2.7 -> 2.3
        (2.2), reversed 6.0 -> 4.8 (5.9), at 400 after a stop 15.3 -> 16.1 %, speed 231.5 -> 234.2,
        frags a minute even (10.7), lava deaths 5.5 -> 5.9 a match (within noise).
        HEAD TO HEAD (default field, 12 ten-minute seeds a map, each side against `full`): dm2 +1.0
        red / +2.3 blue (mean +1.6 a match); e1m2 +7.4 / -2.7 (mean +2.4). ON (2026-11-12).
        NEXT, FIRST: THE TRICK MAPS (asked 2026-11-12). Four community trick maps, courses of jumps
        where a miss falls into a teleport back to the section's start: `ztricks`, `zjumps`,
        `hoppa2`, `ztrain` (where they are fetched from and their SHA-256s: `.bots/local.md`; they
        are kept out of the tree, installed in the game folder's maps). A LAB (`se-trick`) puts ONE
        bot (an expert's pilot through the keyboard, as in a match) at the start and has it run
        each course to the finish as fast as it can: every section's time, every jump's takeoff
        (place, speed, heading, keys) and landing, and every FAIL (where it fell, which link of the
        route, the takeoff against the link's proof) as tables and as pictures (the course from
        above with the route, each takeoff and landing marked, the fails in red; a chart of speed
        along the course), a digest for the smoke row. Then ITERATE: fix where it fails (the nav's
        proofs on these jumps, the run-ups, the hop, the air control) a map at a time, judged by
        the course's time and its fails, and every fix read back in matches (the sets and head to
        head as above). Then the rest as before: the brief stops' causes left (hits, the hop, the
        rocket jumps' dances), people's stops by place (round 4's second half).
        THE TRICK-MAP LAB, FIRST ITERATIONS (`se-trick`, games/shooters_edge/labs/se_trick_lab.cpp;
        2026-11-12). The course is read off the teleporters (a trigger back to the section's own
        start a FAIL, one to another start the section's exit, chosen by the quickest route with no
        teleporter on it), one expert's pilot through the keyboard runs each section up to
        `--tries` times (bots/pilot_run.hpp `run_route`; the lab watches the triggers), tables
        every section, fail and stall, and draws every section from above (`sections-<map>.png`)
        and the speed along the course. Where the nav proves no way, the pit on the straight way
        to the exit is found (the floor traced down) and its jump proven at a hopper's speed, the
        slowest that lands from 330 u/s up (nav_gaps.hpp; a route takes the quickest flight, so a
        faster jump kept beside it would be the one asked for). ztrain (15 one-pit sections, pits
        232-372 units): the nav alone plans 3, with the pits' jumps 13 (proven at 330-510 u/s),
        through 9 (5 first try), the bot's lip takeoffs 400-430 u/s; the rest STALL: the pilot
        gives the gap trick up ("strafing side to side to its lip", "too fast to reach: planned
        round it": `gap_takeoff`, `gap_off`), plans again, finds no other way and stands idle
        within 0.15-0.6 s. zjumps, hoppa2 (one long section each) and ztricks (a hub of six
        sections) get no further than their first section: one straight line finds one pit, and
        ztricks' order falls back to the nearest trigger where no route is planned.
        NEXT (the trick maps, in order): (1) THE PITS FROM THE FAIL TRIGGERS: each fail floor
        is a pit; the floor nodes along its rims proven across it as jumps at the speed ladder,
        so a winding course of many pits (zjumps, hoppa2) and ztricks' sections plan. (2) A GAP
        NEVER GIVEN UP WHERE NO OTHER WAY IS: when the plan round a gap trick finds nothing, the
        pilot steps back to a run-up behind the lip and goes at it again (as a person does on
        these maps), not idle; the lab's stalls go to 0. (3) The speed the lip asks against what
        the run-up gives (the circle jump, a strafe start, the hop), per section, until ztrain
        runs end to end; then zjumps, hoppa2, ztricks; and each fix read back in matches.
        THE PITS FROM THE FAIL TRIGGERS, FIRST TRY (`--pits=triggers`, the default): a pit's rim
        is the nodes with a drop into a fail trigger; each rim node's three nearest crossings (the
        jump's middle over the trigger, 64-480 units, no walk round as quick) proven at the speed
        ladder. zjumps: 4612 crossings tried, 184 jumps kept (~8 s), still no route: its first
        corridor (`reach-zjumps.png`, the nav's reach drawn; se-reach `--floors` along it) is a
        ledge at 3840 over a pit 512 deep with BLOCKS ~96 units deep stepping down 64 each (3776,
        3712, 3648) 335-384 units apart: a jump onto a block needs ~400 u/s, and at that speed the
        player slides off a 96-unit block before he stops, so no single jump proven to stand on
        its landing holds. People land and jump at once: the proof these courses need is a
        CHAIN, the hop flown block to block (land, jump on the landing's command) and kept as one
        link from the ledge to the floor past the last block. NEXT: (1a) the chain proof
        (nav_gaps.hpp's flight, the jump pressed again on each landing, the view on the next
        block), its link flown by the pilot's hop; then (2) and (3) above.
        THE HOPS PROVEN BY THEIR FLIGHT (`se-trick` second round): (a) a hop across a pit is proven
        by where its jump COMES DOWN (fly_jump, within 48 across and 32 up or down of the far node,
        the pilot's own gap test), not by standing there, and added as a gap link at the slowest
        speed that lands; the pilot's hop then judges each block's next jump on its landing
        (`gap_takeoff`), so a chain needs no link of its own. (b) The rim is found by the floor
        (a node over a pit's trigger with the floor 32 units off it 40+ under its feet): the nav
        links no drop 512 deep, so "a node dropping into the trigger" missed every block. (c) THE
        PITS CLOSED: zjumps' fail triggers hang ~100 units over the pits' floors, so the nav walked
        the floors below them; every node under a flat trigger is closed (links into it cost 1e6).
        (d) One crossing a landing spot (a block's dozen nodes crowded the next block out of a rim
        node's nearest crossings). zjumps: the nav's reach from the start runs through the first two
        corridors' blocks to the junction at the second's end (7080 nodes, `reach-zjumps.png`),
        where the hop into the third corridor is not yet proven; ztrain 10 of 15 through (9 first
        try); ztricks 2 of 7 sections planned. NEXT: the junction's hop on zjumps (higher than its
        takeoff? farther than 600?), then (2) the gap never given up, (3) the lip speeds.
        THIRD ROUND. zjumps' third corridor: its platforms (3264) are narrower than the nav's
        32-unit grid, so no node stands on them (`--spacing=16` gives them nodes, 22251 reached,
        2 min a run, and still no way in: the junction's floor is 160 under them and the second
        corridor's last block lower; how a person enters it is not known yet: read it off a
        recording when one is found). A `--trace` (every command of every try) shows ztrain's
        later sections failing the same way: the circle jump builds 440-450 u/s 110 units short
        of the lip, the jump from there comes down 25 degrees off the gap's line (the curve not
        finished), `gap_takeoff` gives the gap up ("too fast to reach"), the run on loses ~120
        u/s to friction, and a new circle jump carries the bot over the lip without a jump.
        `gappast` (a gap's jump coming down on past its node, on its floor and line, lands; not
        in a preset): ztrain with the pits on the straight way 9 -> 10 through, with the rims'
        hops even (10). The pits' floors marked as harm (`--harm=1`, the lips as brinks): 10 -> 3
        (a hop's stretch ends short of every lip): not kept. NEXT for ztrain: the circle jump
        aimed to FINISH on the gap's line at the lip (its curve begun where its exit heading is the
        jump's), and no ground command over a lip without the jump pressed (a person never walks
        off a trick map's lip); then the gap never given up.
        FOURTH ROUND. `gappast` widened: a gap's jump coming down on the far floor (32 up or down)
        past its node or beside it, within 160, lands: ztrain 10 -> 11 through, all 11 on the
        first try, fails 27 -> 20. `lipjump` (a hop on the ground about to run off its floor at
        200+ jumps there; judging): fires, moves nothing (the jump at the lip is too slow: 359).
        The exit's route may end a step short of a thin trigger when no node touches it: ztrain
        15 of 15 sections planned. What is left (sections 12-15, pits 332-372 needing 480-510
        u/s): the circle jump from the section's start peaks at ~460 u/s 50 units short of the
        lip, the run on the ground to it loses ~60, the gap is given up and the bot runs off the
        lip. NEXT: THE RUN-UP A TRICK NEEDS: when the speed a gap's jump was proven at is more
        than a circle jump from here gives (~450), step back first to the far end of the floor
        behind (the platform's back wall) and come at the lip with the circle jump and a hop or
        two (people's hop starts, `recipes`), taking off at the proven speed; judged by ztrain
        end to end, then zjumps' and hoppa2's pits.
        Tried first in the lab alone (`--backup=1`: every try from the floor's far end, up to 400
        units behind): worse, 11 -> 10 through, 3 on the first try (fails 20 -> 34): the room is
        not what is missing, the pilot does not build a speed out of it. The run-up a gap needs
        is the pilot's to plan (the hops to its speed, then the lip), not a place to start from.
        WHY: traced, the hop's first jump from the far end is judged "a hop that lands this side"
        by a flight forecast with forward held at the gap's landing, but the hop strafes in the
        air, gains, and comes down in the pit. `lipjump` now also asks a hop "this side" of a gap
        to come down 64 units short of the lip (pilot_gaps.cpp `kHopShort`): ztrain with
        `+gappast,+lipjump`: 11 through from each start, 12 from the far end (10 first try,
        fails 18). NEXT: the forecast flown as the hop flies (the side key and the view on the
        velocity, its gain), then the run-up planned by the pilot.
        `gappast` IN MATCHES (default field, 12 ten-minute seeds a map, each side against `full`):

        through (12 -> 6 from the far end): the forecast takes the view and the keys as instant,

        way): the falls left are not misjudged hops. LEFT, sections 14 and 15: the takeoff at

        wall; and past 320 the curve gains NOTHING (~0 a command under every view; the move gives
        +18 at 338 with the wish at its best angle, ~30 degrees off the velocity). Two causes: the
        keys pressed under another view are the curve's wish turned and cut to the eight sets,
        which at 45 degrees give ~0; and the keys land 1-3 commands after they are chosen while
        the best angle moves ~4 degrees a command. `circlekeys` (the set the ground move carries
        furthest, as `airkeys` in the air; judging, in no preset): circle takeoffs 344 -> 356,
        but still ~0 past 320; head to head dm2 +1.3 red / -7.8 blue (mean -3.2), e1m2 +0.1 /
        +1.5 (mean +0.8), speed even: NOT kept. People hold the keys and turn the VIEW: forward
        and a side key held, the view on the velocity, so the wish lies 45 degrees off it, which
        gains in a wide window (+13 a command at 330), and a hand behind the turn only moves the
        wish toward the best angle. `circleview` (built, judging): under the mouse hand the view
        on the velocity's heading and the keys held; first read: the hand's view still 26
        degrees off the velocity and the gain ~0 past 320 (the hand's swing at the curve's start
        overshoots, the keyboard's side key lands late). Led 12 degrees into the turn (25 was
        worse): the hand's circles take off at 381 (from 346; people's starts 391), but the
        aim's stay ~330. `circleview` HEAD TO HEAD (fixed keys, lead 12): dm2 -3.2 red / +10.6
        blue (mean +3.7), e1m2 -6.3 / +2.9 (mean -1.7), speed net -2.4 and +4.4: even. Its
        curve's first commands lost speed (268 -> 227 in 5, no wall: the hand still ~50 degrees
        off, forward and the side key ~95 degrees off the velocity) and the hop's 240 floor ended
        it, so its keys became the ground chooser's (circles ending in a walk 49 -> 33 %).
        `circleaim` (a circle only under the hand's own view: none while aiming, a holder's spot
        watched given up while one curves). The two HEAD TO HEAD as a pair: dm2 -0.8 / +1.3
        (mean +0.3), e1m2 -6.2 / +1.1 (mean -2.5), speed net -0.9 and -1.2; with half the field
        on, its circle takeoffs 342 -> 347. DROPPED, all three in no preset: the circle starts
        too few hops to move a match. The start's speed is the copied starts' (`recipes`: 364,
        p90 439) and the landings' (bots land at 241, people at 347): next for the chain, what
        the air loses before a landing (collisions 29 %, brakes 16 %).
        WHAT A HOP'S FLIGHT LOSES (2026-11-12): every air command of a traced dm2 match split into
        what the air move gives for the keys sent under the view sent (the move's air accelerate
        recomputed off the trace: exact, p50 error 0.01 units/s) and the rest (walls, bodies,
        hits): py/airloss.py, bymode.py, flcause.py in ~/.claude/scratch/bots-work/land. A flight
        off a chained landing (291 of 575 flights at 250+) took off at 324 and landed at 176: the
        keys -57, the walls -80 (a hit of 60+ in one command in 38 %), grazes -11. Of the keys'
        loss, two thirds came AFTER THE HOP HAD ENDED IN THE AIR (its stretch's end `hop_end` near,
        or off the way: 220 a 3-minute match): the walk's forward key toward its target, turned under
        the aim's or the calm view, braked 2.6-3.5 units/s a command. People and bots by one rule
        off se-moves' tracks (py/trackfl.py; flights of 0.3-1.2 s from 250+, no blast): people's
        land at 360 from 408 (clean flights +1 p50), bots' at 188 from 324 (clean flights -63);
        both collide in about a third of their flights (people 32 %, bots 31 %), but people's hits
        graze (landing at 248 after one), the bots' meet the wall square (86).
        `airwalk` (bot.cpp: a walk in the air past 100 units/s pressed with the air's key set under
        the view the keys land under that keeps the velocity on round toward the walk's target, as
        the hop's air, charged for a wall; the pilot's `walk_air`, `walk_air_yaw`): a chained hop
        lands at 257 (from 176), its keys -12 a flight (from -57), the hop's gain p50 -30 -> -7
        (expert) and -22 -> -4 (average), the team 225 -> 237 units/s (one 3-minute dm2 match).
        HEAD TO HEAD (12 ten-minute seeds a map, each side against the default): dm2 -1.8 red /
        +8.2 blue (mean +3.2), e1m2 -2.0 / +4.8 (mean +1.4); the side with it faster, net of the
        sides' skew, dm2 +3.6 and e1m2 +3.1 units/s: ON. Left in a hop's flight: the walls (-88 a
        chained flight): at a hit the route's next node lies ~110 degrees off the velocity, the
        velocity has not turned in the 11 commands before it, and the keys in them are NONE (the
        air's chooser finds no set better than none): next, why (se-team's trace now has `way`,
        the heading the air steers for).
        `lipjump` IN MATCHES (the fifth round's build, 12 ten-minute seeds a map, each side
        against the default): dm2 +5.9 red / -5.2 blue (mean +0.4), lava deaths 14.1 -> 11.3 and
        10.8 a match; e1m2 +4.6 / +7.2 (mean +5.9): ON in every preset.
        THE QUIET RUN ALONG A WALL (asked 2026-11-12): walking with a strafe key pressed into a flat
        wall is a common way to gain speed WITHOUT JUMPING, so without the jump's sound: a person
        reaching an item unheard runs along a wall that way (the wall takes the wish's part into it,
        the part along it keeps accelerating past a walk). A bot that wants to arrive unheard (an
        item it sneaks to, a flank) runs it where a wall lies along its way; judged by the speed it
        reaches against a plain walk on the same stretch (a staged run first, then se-people's
        measure of people along walls) and by whether it is heard less.
      - **0q, the expert's ledger of each enemy's health, and the axe finish** (asked
        2026-11-10): an expert knows the game in and out, so it KEEPS EACH ENEMY'S HEALTH AND
        ARMOUR as a person does, off what its client can know, never off the server's truth:
        the damage its own weapons did (a rocket's or a scattergun's hit to the point; the
        grenade launcher's, the nailguns' and the lightning gun's harder to count but close:
        an expert knows how many seconds its beam was on him, so its guess is within 1-3
        cells' worth, and within a few nails), the armour that took its share by the armour's
        kind, the items he was seen or heard taking, his pain and death cries, and a respawn's
        100 and no armour; it grows `FoeRead::body` (now pickups heard and -20 a cry) into
        this ledger, with an error that grows with what it did not see. A health item SEEN
        taken adds its health to his line (a mega's 100 too, over 100), an armour seen taken
        sets his armour. In a team game (2on2 and up) an enemy LOST FROM SIGHT is forgotten
        (back to unknown: he may have picked up anything, and the sounds round him are
        anyone's); in a DUEL he is kept, and the sounds count for more (only one opponent can
        be the one heard taking the mega or the armour, so a pickup heard is his). Then THE
        AXE FINISH:
        an expert who believes an enemy within reach will die to one axe hit (20, 80 under the
        quad) switches to the axe and kills him with it, as people do to humiliate, rather
        than spend a rocket. BY SKILL: an expert keeps the ledger above and takes the axe
        finish; an INTERMEDIATE keeps a rough one (its own hits counted, no armour sum, no
        cell-by-cell beam) and, once it has lost sight of a player and hears a health pack
        taken, takes him for back at full health; a NOVICE tracks no one's health at all.
        Only experts finish with the axe.
        Yardstick: the ledger's error against the server's truth (the lab knows it; the bot
        never reads it), by weapon (BUILT 2026-11-20: se-team `team.ledger.{unseen,seen}.{miss,
        bias}` and `truth-mean`; dm2, six seeds: the belief misses by 51 points out of sight and
        48 in it, 24 and 8 under the truth; the typical live enemy's body 115. `ledgerforget` ON:
        an enemy neither seen nor heard for 3 s is the typical 115 again: the bias out of sight
        -24 -> -8, the miss 51 -> 49 (the bot's own hits not counted yet), head to head -0.4.
        THE FLOOR (`team.ledger.*.prior-miss`, a belief that always says 115): 54 in sight, 52
        out of it, against the ledger's 49 and 50: THE LEDGER BARELY BEATS KNOWING NOTHING.
        Judged off: `ledgerhits` (its own rockets followed from the muzzle to the burst, the
        splash his believed place takes, x0.8 for armour): the miss in sight 48 -> 50, the bias
        -7 -> -15, head to head -1.9; `ledgerheal` (a health box heard +20): the miss 48.7 ->
        49.6. In a 4on4 a bot's own hits are a small part of what befalls an enemy; what it can
        know is little. NEXT for 0q, before the axe finish: read the miss by what the bot last
        knew of him (seen hurt, heard a cry, a fresh spawn) to find which belief is worth keeping,
        and try the DUEL (one enemy: every sound is his) where the ledger can know.
        BY ITS NEWS (`team.ledger.by.<news>.*`): 77 % of the samples have no news of him (the
        typical body); a death heard is the one news that pays (miss 26 against the floor's 37),
        a pickup heard 46 / 48, a cry 49 / 51. `ledgerobits` ON (an enemy's obituary off the kill
        feed sets his line to a fresh spawn): the spawn's news 4 -> 16 % of the samples, the miss
        in sight 48.3 -> 46.1, its bias -6.7 -> -1.3, head to head -1.1. `spawnkept` ON (a
        spawn's line kept 10 s out of sight, not 3): the spawn's line 16 -> 36 % of the samples
        at a 25-point miss, the miss out of sight 48.8 -> 46.5, in sight 45.8 (the floor 54),
        head to head +1.0. NEXT for 0q: the duel; then the axe finish only where the ledger's
        line is a spawn's or its own count (a 25-point miss), never on the typical body.); people's axe kills a match and the health their victims
        had (off the recordings), the bots' the same; frags head to head (an axe tried on a
        wrong guess costs a kill, so the guess must be good before the axe is drawn).
      - **0s, THE PROJECTILE'S LEAD AGAINST A PERSON** (asked 2026-11-12, after a list of
        prediction techniques from the web). The aim already extrapolates the velocity the server
        sends (read a reaction late by an expert: `eye`), solves the intercept by the flight time
        (a target in the air on his own arc; the grenade's lob), reacts by skill (0.17-0.30 s, half
        when pre-aimed), wanders by skill, leads by skill (none for a novice), aims a far rocket at
        the feet, watches the ways in and fires down lanes (`watch`, `spam`). A Kalman filter is
        not needed (the velocity is sent exact), nor volleys spread between no lead and full lead
        (a fairness device people do not use). The gap is the ROCKET AGAINST A MOVING PERSON:
        direct hits people 15.3 %, bots 10-11 %. Three switches, each judged first in a rocket
        drill (`se-team --drill=rockets`, direct and splash hit rates by skill), then head to
        head on dm2 and e1m2, against people's 15.3 %:
        (1) `leadwall`: the lead point slides along walls as the move does (the player's box
        traced along the predicted path, clipped where it meets one): today a 0.5-1 s lead runs
        straight on into the wall a person must turn along.
        (2) `leadmean`: against a dodger, a long flight is led on his velocity averaged over about
        the flight's time (his swing's middle), a short one on the current: a person turns back
        every 0.3-0.5 s, so the full current velocity misses after his next turn. Read first off
        recordings: where people's rockets were aimed against their targets' current and mean
        velocities.
        (3) `leadarc`: a hopper's curve projected by his turn rate (the heading's change over the
        last commands), not a straight line: hops curve 100-200 degrees a second, and a midair
        rocket at 400+ units/s misses by metres.
        FIRST READINGS (rocket drill, six ten-minute seeds a map, every bot with the switch; direct
        hits novice / average / expert, frags a minute): the default dm2 7.6 / 9.7 / 11.8 %,
        11.3; e1m2 8.9 / 11.1 / 15.0 %, 12.0. `leadwall` (bot.cpp `lead_slide`: the box traced a
        step up, what is left past a wall projected along it, two bumps): dm2 9.1 / 10.7 / 12.5 %,
        rockets at a running target 17.9 -> 20.1 %, frags 13.6, damage 259 -> 277; e1m2 (open)
        8.9 / 11.6 / 14.7 %, frags 12.5. HEAD TO HEAD (12 ten-minute seeds a map, each side
        against the default): dm2 +2.7 red / -1.8 blue (mean +0.4), e1m2 -0.4 / +0.3 (mean 0.0),
        speed net +1.2 and +1.1: no cost, the rockets' direct hits nearer people's: ON. `leadmean` (the drift, the course
        averaged over 0.5 s, weighed in as the flight nears 0.5 s): dm2 8.5 / 10.2 / 11.3 %, e1m2
        12.2 / 10.4 / 11.1 %: the novices' hits rise, the experts' fall (a drift half a second
        old lags an expert target's turns): NOT kept, in no preset; with `leadwall` e1m2 10.2 /
        11.7 / 15.1 %. People's own rockets read off recordings before the drift is tried again.
        `leadarc` (the course's turn over 0.15 s past 200 units/s, the way across on that arc for
        at most 90 degrees): dm2 7.3 / 9.8 / 11.9 %, e1m2 9.7 / 11.8 / 14.3 %; rockets at a fast
        target 16.0 -> 16.3 % and 15.8 -> 16.7 %, frags and damage a little down; with `leadwall`
        no better than `leadwall` alone: NOT kept, in no preset (the drill's targets hop little;
        tried again against people's hops, the 0t sessions).
        Later, with 0q's ledger: each enemy's own habits (which way he dodges a shot, where he
        spends his time) kept by an expert as a person learns an opponent.
      - **0t, A BOT AT MY CONTROLS, LIVE** (asked 2026-11-12): the maintainer turns on a setting
        in his own game client and the bot takes over his player, his view and his keys, on a
        live server, so he watches on his own screen how a bot reacts to real people and debugs
        it as it plays. The same bot the matches run (the fighter, its pilot, its hands and its
        keyboard, its features and skill as settings), fed by what his client knows (the
        client-fed path `se-team --client-fed=1` already proves), its commands sent as his
        player's. FOR TESTERS ONLY: the server allows it only to players it names (a list on our
        own server; any other server refuses it, and a client never turns it on without the
        server's yes), so it is never an aimbot in a stranger's game. ANNOUNCED: the moment it
        is turned on (and off) the server tells every player in the game, in a game's words,
        that a bot is playing that player now, and his name carries a mark while it does. Any
        key or mouse movement of his own (or one bind) takes the controls back at once. While it
        plays, the client RECORDS A DEMO of the session (the replay file, `.ged`, beside the
        log, its clock the log's, so `se-why`, `se-people` and `se-moves` read the same moments
        off both, and a moment can be watched again), the bot's decision log is written as a
        match's is (`se-why` reads it), and a
        small overlay shows its mode, goal and the decision that last changed, so a moment seen
        live is answered from the log. Yardstick: a session played with testers, the log read
        back for the moments he marks; and the bot's measures in it against the same bot in our
        own matches (does it behave the same against people as against bots).
      - **0u, THE PILOT ON THE GAME'S OWN MOVE** (asked 2026-11-12, after main's M18v8: the map
        nav, the traces and the arsenal on either game; M18v.md's open "the pilot plans with the
        first game's move"). The bots' movement still assumes Quake's: the nav's link
        proofs and the pilot's forecasts call Quake's step (`player_move`), and the hop's
        work is tuned on its air (the air chooser's 30-unit cap at acceleration 10, `aim_air_set`;
        the circle jump's ground step, `circle_step`; the hop's turn rate for the wall look,
        `hop_turn_rate`, `arc_clear`; the jump's 270 rise and 800 gravity in `jump_blocked`;
        maxspeed 320, friction 4). Quake II's move differs: maxspeed 300, its own friction and
        step, and by default NO air acceleration (the plain accelerate at a tenth in the air, no
        30-unit cap; `q2_jump_rules`), so Quake's strafe jumping does not exist there
        unless its server turns it on. The work: one MOVE MODEL the game hands its bots (its step
        on a copy of the state, its speeds, gravity, jump, friction and air rule:
        Quake's `player_move` or Quake II's step), read by the nav's proofs, the pilot's
        forecasts and every key chooser in place of the constants above; each hop feature
        (`hop`, `airkeys`, `aimhop`, `airview`, `airwall`, `chainhop`, the circle) asking the
        model whether its air allows it, off where it does not. Yardstick: Quake's
        bots bit-identical (their digests unchanged, as `first_arsenal()` held them); on the
        Quake II `se-q2-bots` with fewer wall slides (M18v8c's open item), its links proven by
        its own move (a jump Quake II's move cannot make no longer planned), and its server
        with air acceleration on, the bots' hop measured there as on Quake's.
      - **0v, THE BUNNY HOP AS A PLAYER DOES IT** (asked 2026-11-12, after watching a bot match):
        the bots wait too long on the ground before jumping again and lose the speed; they press
        forward during the hop; they jump into the wall, not away from it. Measured on a traced
        3-minute dm2 match: after a hop's landing the ground lasts p50 21 commands (0.27 s), the
        first ground frame jumps in 2 %; forward held in 19 % of the hop's air commands and 42 % of
        its ground ones; of 577 hop landings 154 still hop (the rest walk: 87 % of the hops that end
        on a landing come down under `hop_slow`'s 240 units/s), and those press the jump on the
        ground, the key down 2-3 commands later. THE WAY A PLAYER DOES IT, each a switch, judged
        head to head and by the ground at a landing, the air's gain and the takeoff's speed:
        (1) `hopjump`: the jump RELEASED on the way up and HELD on the way down, so it goes off on
        the landing frame (no ground friction); a hop that lands slow jumps again too (the air's
        side key gains most at a low speed), ending only where the stretch ends.
        (2) `hopside`: NO FORWARD KEY at any time in the hop (its air and its landing): the strafe
        key alone, the one the mouse moves toward (the view turning right: the right key).
        (3) `hopopen`: the takeoff and the curve toward the open space, away from a wall: the side
        whose arc stays clear; a start from a run along a wall (speed taken there, the quiet run
        above), the mouse circled out toward the corridor's space, the jump there, forward let go.
        BUILT (2026-11-12): `hopjump` (pilot.cpp: the jump held on the way down past `hop_slow`'s
        240, so it goes off on the landing frame; a hop given a new route in the air, off its way or
        a new goal, hops on along it: those ended 520 of ~700 hops in the air), `hopside` (no
        forward: the hop's own keys and bot.cpp's air sets the strafe key alone, the side the view
        turns toward). Read (3-minute dm2 matches, both on): the landing's jump on the first ground
        frame 2 -> 33 %, forward in the hop's air 18 -> 5 %, the team 237 / 233 -> 241 / 241
        units/s. Hopping on from a slow landing cost 23 units/s (the air's side key gains less
        than a run): kept to landings past 240. HEAD TO HEAD (both, 12 ten-minute seeds a map,
        each side against the default): dm2 +6.2 red / -1.7 blue (mean +2.3), the side with it
        +2.7 units/s; e1m2 +0.2 / +0.5 (mean +0.3), +0.8 units/s: BOTH ON. The trace's flights split at a ground row: a jump on the landing frame shows
        none, so a chain reads as one flight (split them at the jump's rise next). `hopopen`
        (pilot.cpp: the wall-in-the-arc side swap for every skill, a circle's side out to the open
        when the way is near straight ahead): head to head dm2 -4.4 red / +1.9 blue (mean -1.3),
        e1m2 +6.1 / -4.3 (mean +0.9), the side with it slower (-1.9, -3.7 units/s), its hop's wall
        hits no fewer: NOT kept, in no preset (the hits come under the aim's view, which no side
        choice of the pilot's steers). `airarc`
        (the air's sets flown held through a look) halved the wall hits but braked as much: head
        to head dm2 -5.6, e1m2 +2.1: NOT kept.
      - **0w, THE VIEW LEVEL, NO WALKING BACKWARDS** (asked 2026-11-12, after the same match): the
        bots look down often and walk backwards. A person looks straight ahead, and walks
        backwards ONLY when chased and firing at the enemy chasing him. Measured (the same match):
        the view more than 10 degrees down in 24 % of the commands alive (the aim in a fight 11 k,
        a spot watched while roaming 6 k, the aim while roaming 3 k); the back key on the ground in
        10 % (walking the route under a spot watched 1.8 k, under the aim in a fight 1.6 k and
        roaming 0.9 k, under the calm view 0.8 k). Switches: (1) `levelview`: a spot watched and
        the calm view held level (the pitch toward 0 unless the spot is well above or below and
        near); the aim's feet shot only when it fires; (2) `noback`: the view watched never turned
        more than ~100 degrees off the way walked (the watch given up, or the way turned to), the
        back key kept for a retreat firing at a chaser. Yardstick: people's pitch and back-key
        shares off recordings (se-people), the bots' by one rule, head to head.
        PEOPLE AND BOTS BY ONE RULE (se-moves tracks, py/viewback.py): running backwards (on the
        ground at 150+, the velocity more than 120 degrees off the view) people 13.8 %, bots 22.3 %;
        the pitch: people look down MORE on the whole (p50 9 degrees, bots 2) but the bots' tail is
        steeper (past 45 degrees down 4.0 % against 1.9 %). The tail was A BUG: a look let go is
        paced back at 90 degrees a second in pitch (about 1.2 a command), inside the mouse hand's
        3-degree tolerance, so the hand never stroked and a bot ran about looking 79 degrees down
        for 35 s. `levelview` (the tolerance under the pace's step; a spot watched within 15
        degrees of level): past 45 down 4.6 -> 1.9 % (people 1.9), past 30 down 8.9 -> 5.4 % (people
        6.1), speed 234 -> 236 (six 3-minute dm2 seeds). `noback` (moving, no spot watched or fired
        at, and roaming no player newly seen, more than 100 degrees off the way walked): backwards
        21.0 -> 20.5 % only: the rest is FIGHTS (the aim in a fight walking the route 4 %, a retreat
        1.5 %); next for it, a fight's way walked turned toward the enemy unless he chases.
        HEAD TO HEAD (`noback` and `levelview` together, 12 ten-minute seeds a map, each side

        speed even: BOTH ON.

        `paceback` (A BUG: an aim lost stays `aiming` until the view is back on the way, and the

        backwards 18.4 % against people's 13.8 (runs of 0.25 s+ 10.7 against 8.8); the fight's
        own back key is the frags' (every way of taking it away cost 2-8 a match). The short
        roaming blips (under 0.25 s) are mostly the calm view's about-face a few commands behind
        a `uturn`'s velocity (the route ahead of the velocity, the view 120+ off it): left, the
        view already turning faster than people's.
      - **0k2, the hide**: experts and intermediates switch to the scattergun right after each
        launcher or arc shot and back for the next (timed with the refire), so a death drops a
        pack without the launcher; novices keep it up. Experts who know they will die finish with
        the scattergun. Switch `hide`. Yardstick: the weapon in hand at death by skill, launcher
        packs dropped a match, the enemy's launcher-owned share (people 33 %), head to head.
        MEASURED (2026-11-12, se-kills `hide.*` off recordings, people and bots by one rule; the
        bots' recordings now carry the frags and the dead and gibbed bits, so their kills match
        a killer: before, 72 deaths of ~140 found and none matched): of the launcher's owners
        killed, the launcher in hand: dm2 people 69 % (51 recordings, 2776 owners), bots 59 %
        (four 10-minute seeds, 78); e1m2 people 45 % (44, 2201), bots 71 % (65); the plain
        scattergun in hand dm2 27 / 26 %, e1m2 42 / 14 %. The gap is e1m2's alone; the owners
        are a larger share of the kills on dm2 for people (29 %) than bots (17 %). With ROCKETS
        LEFT (`hide.*` counts only owners with rockets: none, and the scattergun is forced): the
        launcher in hand dm2 people 72 %, bots 79 %; e1m2 49 / 87 %; the plain scattergun dm2 25 /
        10 %, e1m2 39 / 8 %: the bots do not hide. OUR MOD holds a weapon select until the refire
        is over (a launcher fired stays in hand 0.8 s) and takes it at once after, so the hide is
        between fights. `hide` (fighter.cpp `hide_launcher`: the launcher's owner, not a novice,
        nobody in sight, takes the scattergun up; a sighting takes the launcher back at once, past
        the impulse gap): dm2 73 / 25 %, e1m2 56 / 31 % (people 72 / 25, 49 / 39); head to head

      - **0d, the arc in the water**: fired waist deep it discharges round the firer, killing
        him too. Never by mistake; THE SACRIFICE: a bot fresh from a spawn with the arc, in the
        water, discharges on an enemy quad or launcher bearer it would kill, never with a mate
        in reach (switch `discharge`, a staged test, a team line). Judge on dm3. Yardstick:
        discharges, bearers killed, mates killed (zero), head to head.
      - **0e, the brain**: each bot's own store of what a player could know (who has the
        launcher, the quad and until when, each enemy's estimated health and armour, item timers,
        where enemies were last seen and heard, the team's lines, its own deaths), each fact with
        its source, time and a fading confidence. What a bot can store is its skill (a novice
        times nothing; an expert times every key item and counts rockets); what it does with it
        is a second axis. First: every place a bot reads the server's truth (items' `present`,
        `foes` by entity, `rl_fired`, `last_seen_*`, `knowledge.hpp`'s timers) moved behind the
        brain's query with a skill gate. Yardstick: `se-team --brain=<file>` (each belief beside
        the truth), expert against average brains head to head, item control.
      - **1, ask the recordings how people play it** (a demo study tool and a `demo-study`
        skill): a question about play answered by a query over people's recordings,
        parameterised by area (a `.loc` place or a point and radius), item and its clock, time
        window and players' state, reporting who is there, where they stand and look, the ways
        in, arrivals against the respawn, kills by weapon, the moves; the same query on the bots'
        recordings beside it, and situations (e.g. "low on health in the RA's area, survives and
        kills N") found and drawn from above. Built on `se-people` and `client/demo_scan.hpp`.
        - **1a, how people come to an item**: every pickup's path over the 15 s before, clustered
          into the few ways people take, each with its share and success, drawn and tabled beside
          the bots'; the winning ways become routes or rules.

        - **1b, the hop that builds speed** (part done: measured, wall stops, `headroom`,
          `liphop`, the circle jump). FOUND 2026-11-10 (se-moves `--dir`, one rule over both:
          51 people's dm2 matches against six 10-minute bot matches): the ground between jumps is

          dm2's YA pit north-west corner (2271-2304, 192-224) the run drifts 30 units south of its
          links into, the doors at 2688 0, plat 206 (2432 -1000), 2048 0. A proven link is clear;
          the line a bot runs at 400 u/s is not, and the walk has no look for a solid ahead (the
          hop has `arc_clear`). DONE (2026-11-10, feature `ahead`): THE WALK'S LOOK
          AHEAD. Each bump is now put down to what took the speed (`team.bumps.by-*`, `by=` on
          the log's `speed_lost`): of ~21 a bot-minute, 12.7 walls, 6.6 PLAYERS (bots running
          into each other), 1 hits' knockback, 0.4 movers. Most wall bumps are one corner
          clipped at 320 u/s: the pursuit's wish turns past the corner and never takes the old
          way's speed off. The walk's aim is pulled back until the box reaches it straight, and
          where a sweep along the velocity meets a wall the keys are FLOWN THROUGH THE MOVE
          (`player_move` over the map's solids, the hands' delay first) and a way that does not
          bump is pressed (the pursuit, the turn braked, the next node, no keys). Six 3-minute
          seeds: wall bumps 12.7 -> 10.3 a bot-minute, speed 192 -> 194, frags a minute 9.1 ->
          9.8; the 10-minute set on seeds 1-12 put HEAD TO HEAD at -6 frags a match, so it was
          held off the presets until read. READ (2026-11-10): the -6 was one side's draw. Side by
          side (`--features-red=+ahead` and `--features-blue=+ahead` against the sides' own skew,
          seeds 1-12 and 13-24): +1.2 and -6.4, then +1.5 and +5.2: +0.4 frags a match, EVEN;
          its side's wall bumps 12.0 -> 9.8 a bot-minute, its speed no lower (177-183 against
          176). Foresight off while aiming (half the `foresee` changes are in fights) was worse
          (-3.8). `ahead` ON in every preset. Dropped on
          the way: a lean off the wall a straight sweep meets, and braking every turn by the
          side key (both cost speed: 172-178). DONE (2026-11-10, feature `bodies`): OTHER
          PLAYERS' BOXES IN THE FORESIGHT (`look_ahead.hpp` `OtherBox`): the players the client
          was sent within 400 units flown as boxes moved along their velocities, flown when one
          comes within touch on both velocities; and A BODY ON THE WAY (`body_on_way`): a player
          standing on the walk's straight line walked round beside and past him on the clear
          side, or waited for where there is no room (a third of the player bumps were the same
          bot walking into the same standing mate again within 3 s: seed 1, red3 17 times in 11 s
          into a mate camping by plat 206; repeats 148 -> 102, none past 5 at a spot; the
          companion's exit signal, 2 jumps without it, 15 with it). 24 ten-minute dm2 seeds, one
          side with it: its player bumps 5.4 -> 4.4 a bot-minute, its speed +6 u/s, head to head
          -3.9, -0.8, +8.8, +2.6 = +1.6 frags a match; on both sides player bumps 5.5 -> 4.1,
          speed 174 -> 182. ON in every preset. se-team now
          gives each side's speed and wall and player bumps (`team.side.<side>.speed-avg`,
          `bumps.by-wall-per-bot-min`, `bumps.by-player-per-bot-min`) under `--sides=1`. DONE (2026-11-10): THE
          PLATS, ON THE MAINTAINER'S RULE "stay clear of the platform until it has come down and is
          silent; you rise a step walking onto it": a plat is stepped onto only at its rest and
          still for 0.3 s (`silent`, each mover's height noted a command), and waited for outside
          its box till then; a node on a plat is reached only with the feet at its top as it is
          now (the step up; it was reached at any height, so a bot run in under the raised slab
          "passed" the plat's node and walked its top's links into the legs, 6 bumps in 4 s); a
          plan never starts from a plat's node under it raised (`plan_from`: the nearest node off
          it that a straight walk reaches within `lost`; the nearest by the columns was across a
          wall under plat 96 and a bot planned again every other command for 8 minutes). 12
          ten-minute dm2 seeds, both sides: speed 182 -> 203 u/s, frags a minute 8.5 -> 10.4,
          waits 382 -> 197 a match, time stood at a mover 7.4 -> 5.0 %; head to head the side
          with it +16 and +19 u/s, +9.1 and -4.3 frags = +2.4 a match. NEXT, FIRST: the remaining wall bumps (the YA bars' south post at
          2688 -75). Then: hop INTO a teleporter (end the stretch at its
          trigger, not 256 before: drop links refused 111 times for a teleporter ahead); THE
          HOP'S VIEW KEEPING ITS SCAN (`liphop`'s cost: its side sights fewer enemies while the
          view rides the velocity); THE HOP IN A FIGHT (62 % of the fast walking is in a fight,

        - **1c, the map's common routes from people's recordings**: every player's path binned

          items and enemies ahead). Yardstick: the bots' heat map against people's, time on
          never-used parts, head to head.
      - **1b, THE BOT TOOLS OWED** (the `bots` skill's No wishlist: what a session lacked, built
        next): (a) se-team's trace and decision log keyed by the player's name and on the
        recording's clock (now the trace's bot index is not the recording's slot, it runs ~0.5 s
        ahead of the recording, and its z is the feet: every join by hand matched bots by their
        position); (b) se-team and se-people count passes by key item spot (a player lacking it
        passes within 250 units while it lies there: taken or left, and for the left the role,
        mode and goal) and a short goal's end (taken, lost to another, died, given up): written
        three times as scratch scripts on 2026-11-19 (passes30.py, passwhy.py, the detours'
        outcomes).
      - **2, a bot match streamed as it plays**: `se-team --stream=<dir>` writes a block of the
        running metrics, the deaths by place, each bot's mode and goal and a map snapshot every
        match-minute, so a match gone wrong is stopped early. Yardstick: a six-seed A/B's first
        wrong sign caught in its first minutes.

        LINE (near from below); `routetime` (the run by the reach's route time) made the YA lie

        lever is not the score: a team's taker for it (items 3-4: who is sent, `need ya`, the
        holder's post), its trip held to the end. `routetime` in no preset.
        THE TAKER BUILT (2026-11-13, `fetch`, mind.cpp): every lying sample had someone alive who

        A player whose armour is worse, as the team's armour comes up (believed there or due in

        the quad: the item score's (the goal engine, item 11), not the climb's any more. `ya up` said by who sees it come back and a needing mate sent;
        averages time the armours roughly; the holder lets a hurt mate through. Yardstick: lying

      - **4, needs said to the team**: a free player says `need rl`/`need ya`/`need ra`, a mate
        who sees the item points to it (`point ya`), and the needy one goes. Yardstick: the wait

      - **5, enemy status, estimated** (part of 0e): health, armour and weapons from what a player

        cannot be told from his mates later). Yardstick: the estimate against the truth in
        sight and at 1, 3 and 10 s after.

        MEASURED AGAIN (2026-11-13, e1m2, se-people's rule: an enemy in a clear line anywhere

        THE VIEW AGAINST THE RUN'S TURN (se-moves `--track`, eight people's e1m2 recordings and

        PEOPLE'S VIEW BY SPOT (`viewbook`, shooter/view_book.hpp, se-team `--viewbook=<file>`): a
