      - **6b, THE GLANCE AT A KEY ITEM'S SPOT** (asked 2026-11-20): running past where a key item
        spawns (the launchers, the red and yellow armours, the mega, the quad and the pent) with
        the spot in a clear line from its eye, a player looks at it quickly to see whether the
        item is there, and looks back to his run. An expert does it every time he passes such a
        spot; an average player more often than not; a novice about one pass in five (the odds a
        skill trait, read off people's recordings by level where they allow, the maintainer's
        figures until then). What it is for: an item is learned only when its spot is on the
        bot's screen (knowledge.cpp, `sees_player` in the view's cone), so a bot that runs past
        the launcher with its view on the run never learns it lies there or that it was taken;
        the glance fills the item's belief (there, gone, the time it went) as a person's does.
        BUILD: a switch `itemglance`; the chance by skill drawn off the fighter's own stream once
        a pass (one spot a pass, the item worth most to it, not one seen in the last few
        seconds); not in a fight or with an enemy in sight or heard near, not while a rocket
        jump, a gap trick or a hop's air view owns the view; the look through the one hand
        (`engine/ai/mouse.hpp`, `bot_hand_override`), a quick stroke to the spot, held a moment
        (~0.2-0.3 s) and back, its writer named in se-team's trace (`view_by`) and the decision
        log (`item_glance`: the spot, the chance, drawn or not, what it saw). JUDGE: people's
        and the bots' glances by one rule, a new se-people measure (`glance.key.*`: of the
        passes within ~600 units of a key spot with a clear line from the eye and the spot off
        the screen as the pass starts, the share in which the view comes within ~20 degrees of
        it, by level; the bots' read off `se-team --save`), the beliefs' truth (se-team's trace
        `pdue`/`preal` and the false "there" share, now 24 % for the quad), the items lying, and
        head to head as always. Before building: measure people's glances first (how often,
        how long, how far off the run), and count how often the bots pass a key spot with it off
        the screen.
        MEASURED (2026-11-20, se-people `glance.key.*`, glance_read.hpp: a pass within 600 units
        in a clear line, run past 150, never within 96 of the spot; dm2, people's 92 4on4
        recordings against six 10-minute expert matches): passes 9.8 a minute alive (bots 14.0),
        starting with the spot off the screen 55 % (bots 65); of those, the view within 20
        degrees of the spot 26 % (bots 36: the `glance` swipes already cross it), for 0.08 s at
        the median (p90 0.5: people's "glance" is mostly a sweep crossing the spot), the spot on
        the screen at all 41 % (bots 48), on it lying 60+ degrees off the run 33 % (bots 33). Each
        player's own share of glances lies p25-p75 22-29 %: no level glances "every pass" by this
        rule. A pass SHOWS the spot (at its start or later) for people 68 %, the bots 66 %: even
        over all spots; THE GAP IS THE LAUNCHERS: the low RL 96 % (bots 77), the high RL 86 % (48;
        people pass it with it off the screen 27 %, bots 77-82), the YA by the water 58 (49). The
        rest even or the bots over (the pillar YAs 53 / 63 against 66 / 69). e1m2 (44 people's
        recordings): shown 86 % a pass, off at the start 32 %.
        BUILT: `itemglance` (knowledge.cpp `item_glance_offer`, bot.cpp the calm view's
        item swipe, held 0.25 s once on; the decision `item_glance`, the trace's `view` writer
        `itemglance` and column `iglance`, se-team `team.item-glances-per-min`), odds by map sense
        0.2 / 0.6 / 1.0 (the maintainer's). dm2, six seeds both sides with it: 2.3 glances a
        bot-minute; shown a pass, the low RL 76 -> 89 % (people 96), the high RL 48 -> 56 (86), the
        YA by the water 49 -> 58 (58), the quad 53 -> 52 (58: its passes are hopped or fought, the
        calm view rarely has them), all spots 66 -> 69 (68); the pillar YA west 66 -> 70 (people
        53) moved away. Items lying unchanged (RL 38 -> 40 %): the bots' beliefs were already right
        (`team.lying.*.unknown`, nobody believing a lying item there, 0.0-0.4 % for every item), so
        the glance is a human measure, not item control. Head to head (12 seeds, 10 minutes):
        base -2.4, red with it +8.6, blue with it -0.3: +11.0 / -2.1, +4.45 a match on dm2.
        e1m2 (12 seeds): shown a pass, the quad 70 -> 75 % (people 79), the RL 88 -> 90 (95), all
        spots 80 -> 83 (86); head to head base +0.6, red +5.3, blue +1.9: +1.7 a match. But the
        view within 20 degrees of the spot rose AWAY from people (dm2 36 -> 39 %, e1m2 51 -> 63;
        people 26 and 38): people bring a spot onto the screen without centring it. THE SHORT STOP
        (the swipe ends 35 degrees short of the spot, on the screen, not under the crosshair; dm2
        six seeds): shown a pass the low RL 88 %, the high RL 60, all spots 68 (as centred), within
        20 degrees 34 % (below the base's 36). Head to head (12 seeds): dm2 +5.2 / -2.6, +1.3 a
        match; e1m2 +3.8 / -0.7, +1.55. ON (in the preset). Chart: the work folder's
        glance/dm2-glance-by-spot.png. Left: the high RL (60 against people's 86: bots pass it with
        it off the screen 77 %, people 27: people come at it facing it, the run's way, not a
        glance), the quad (passes hopped or fought, the calm view rarely holds them).
      - **6c, A KEY ITEM SEEN IS TAKEN; THE LAUNCHER TIMED AND WAITED ON** (asked 2026-11-20: "if
        the bot sees a key item he should take it; after the RL, stick around, say rl safe, time
        it, rl in 10, and get mates to come to the RL to gain more RLs and control"). MEASURED
        (se-people `launcher.*`, launcher_read.hpp, dm2, people's 92 recordings against six
        10-minute expert matches): launchers taken 2.49 a minute (bots 2.42), but one held 46 %
        of the time (bots 24: the bots lose theirs twice as fast); the taker near its spot over
        the next 20 s 46 % (bots 63: they stay already); mates without one within 600 at its
        return 0.72 (0.62); the next taker a mate without one 36 % (39). THE GAP: from its
        showing to its going people 1.5 s at the median (4.8 mean), the bots 7.9 (19.3); with a
        mate waiting near people 1.0, the bots 7.8; ON the spot (within 200) at its return a mate
        without one 21 % (bots 6.5), an enemy 20 % (3). The bots' talk has it already: a holder
        says `safe [place]` every 20 s, an expert calls a timed key item at 10 s (`rl in 10`),
        mates read both. se-team `team.rl-near.*` (a launcher lying, a bot without one within
        600): believed gone 20 %, fighting 35, going for it 10, going for another item 35 (of
        those, the launcher in sight 5 %, within a 2.5 s run 15: on another floor).
        `seetake` (a key item in sight within a 2.5 s run taken at once, judging): going for it
        10 -> 12 %, the wait's median 7.2 -> 7.1 s, the quad lying 29 -> 39 %, frags a minute
        14.5 -> 13.4 (six seeds): it rarely has anything to act on. NOT KEPT, in no preset.
        `rlfetch` (the launcher's taker, mind.hpp: the nearest without one, within a 12 s run,
        goes to a launcher there or due within 10 s, says `coming rl`, waits on it; judging; six
        seeds): a mate on the spot at its return 6.5 -> 9.8 % (people 21), a launcher held 25 ->
        29 % (46), the launcher lying 40 -> 34 %; but the wait from its showing 7.2 -> 8.7 s, the
        quad lying 29 -> 40 %. 127 trips a match: 21 had it, 38 ended by a role (the quad's rush
        or carry: the high RL's waiter is the quad's nearest), 33 believed taken by another, 32
        dead on the way. NEXT for it: the waiter kept off the quad's rush, the trip's deaths (a
        waiter standing in the open), then head to head.
      - **6d, THE HOLDER POINTS, AND DENIES** (asked 2026-11-20: "holders of the RA point at it
        when it appears and do not take it unless under 100 armour or an enemy comes near; the
        same for the RL and the YA: take the RL if low on rockets with no rocket packs near, else
        point and wait for mates; take it if an enemy comes near; deny the enemy the key
        items"). BUILT `holdpoint` (judging; mind.cpp `holder_leaves`, the holder stands off on a
        node 128-320 from the item in a clear line, fighter.cpp `stand_node`; the point is the
        existing `ra at [ra]`, a mate told rates the item 4 times for 10 s). Six dm2 seeds: it
        runs (with its item up, a holder's plans left it 524 times a match, took it 703), but
        moves nothing: armour worn 45.4 -> 47.0 (people 66.0), a launcher held 25 -> 25 % (46),
        lying RA 59 -> 62 % (people 19), YA 59 -> 59 (38), RL 40 -> 42 (11). Pointed only with a
        mate believed within 600 (else taken: denied): the same (47 / 27 / 60 / 60 / 43). THE
        GAP IS DENIAL ITSELF: people's key items hardly lie (RA 19 %, RL 11 %), the bots' lie
        three to four times as long; the YA lies "unwanted" 78 % (every bot that believes it
        there wears armour as good: the game refuses it), the RA "going" 32 % and "busy" 23 %.
        MEASURED (se-people `show.<item>.*` and its table by spot, show_read.hpp; dm2, people's 95
        recordings against six 10-minute expert matches): the wait from a key item's showing to
        its going, p50 (taken within 1 s), people / bots: the quad 0.9 / 20.6 s (56 / 0 %), the
        north RA 1.9 / 30.2, the ledge RA 2.2 / 17.6 (24 / 2 %), the low RL 1.0 / 5.3 (55 / 11 %),
        the high RL 3.9 / 42.5, the pillar YAs 12 / 37-62, the water YA 4.4 / 5.3. The taker was
        within 600 at the showing (waited for it): the RA 77 / 44 %, the quad 90 / 31. Armour worn
        (`armour.wear.*`): none 48 / 64 %, YA 26 / 20, RA 26 / 16; the points while worn alike.
        The gap is TIMING: people stand on the spot as it comes back. FOUND (the decision log's new
        `role` and `door_errand`): the quad's rush ended 161 of 214 times on "no way to the quad
        known": the way up from the east is the teleporter in the water YA's alcove, behind its
        bars (door 49, the button at 2256,-232); a rusher meeting the bars ran the button's
        errand, the errand kept the bars off its reach, the quad left it, the rush ended, the
        errand ended, and round again. `errandreach` (judging; the errand's door off the routes,
        not the reach; a rush held while the reach is worked out again; a reach of one node not
        known yet): role changes a match 555 -> 314; six seeds, a mate on the quad's spot at its
        showing 3 -> 16 % (people 48), near it 55 -> 74 (87), the wait 20.6 -> 17.2 s. The quad
        lies as long (29 -> 31 %): the rusher's route still changes every think (its goal node
        between two by the quad, the way through the YA's teleporter, off the ledge and back).
      - **6e, ROUTES TO EVERY ITEM FROM EVERYWHERE, DOORS AS ERRANDS** (asked 2026-11-20: "build
        routes from all places in a map to each item so the bot does not need to calculate routes
        on the fly; also through doors that are closed: what buttons to shoot or press"). A field
        per item: a reverse search from the item's node over the nav, each node's time to the item
        and its next link, built once a map (dm2 2479 nodes x ~30 items); a set per mover class
        the reach now switches (on foot, rocket jumps, grenade jumps, gap tricks). A door only a
        button opens is a link through it costing the run to the button, the press or shot and
        the run back, kept when that fits the door's time open; the field stores the errand, so a
        bot knows before it sets off that the north RA is the window gate's shot button (t18,
        2496,-1252) and the way through. Live only: a mover's state, a jump missed twice, danger,
        checked along the stored way, a live route the fallback. The fields' times replace the
        per-second reach. Judged on the waits above (se-people's table by spot), then head to head.
        BUILT (2026-11-20): plugins/shooter/nav_field (tests/shooter_nav_field_test.cpp): a field
        per goal node and jumper, a layered reverse search (layer 0 every button-fired door shut,
        one layer per button with its doors open, the press stepping between them when the way on
        crosses the door within its wait and travel), kept per match (net/qw_team_nav's
        `TeamNav::fields`). The errands (bots/buttons `field_errands`): a touch button from its
        node, a shot one from every node that sees it within 900; only doors that are a way when
        fired (not starting open, not a crusher: dm2's t3 walls by the pillar YAs). `se-reach
        --field='x y z > x y z'` tables the errands, the time per jumper, the way with its press:
        dm2's north RA from the low RL's corridor is 11 s, the window gate's button shot from the
        plat's top (2449,-943,96), through the window (the old reach saw no way: one press node,
        on the floor below, too far to make the gate's 3.7 s). `fields` (judging): an item's
        reach and run off its field; a way that starts with a button begins its errand at once
        (`field_errand`: 52-115 a match). `fieldway` (judging): the pilot's route off the field
        when nothing on it is barred, cut or shut now. Six seeds both sides with `fields`: armour
        worn 45 -> 57 points (people 66), the pillar YAs' wait 37/62 -> 20/25 s, YA lying 59 -> 52
        %, the ledge RA taken within 1 s 2 -> 11 %; the quad 29 -> 38 % (now rated by its run: 15 s
        round on foot from under it, the YA bars' button, the alcove's teleporter, the high ledge,
        a gap trick), frags a minute 14.5 -> 11.7 (both sides run for armour). Head to head (12
        seeds): base +3.3, red with it +2.3, blue with it +3.1: -1.0 / +0.2, -0.4 a match, even;
        frags a minute 14.6 -> 12.6 with either side on it. se-people `deaths-per-min` (new): people
        3.13, the bots 2.36, with `fields` 1.95: the pace moves away from people's while the armour
        comes nearer (people hold their armour AND fight). JUDGING, not in the preset. NEXT: where
        the fields' bots spend the time the fights lost (the errands: 52-115 a match; trips to far
        armour by their true run), the quad from under it (on foot 15 s round; people rocket jump
        from below or take the launcher's corridor jump), then `fieldway`'s set and head to head.
      - **6f, GOALS COMBINED: THE FIGHT ON THE WAY, THE CHASE ONLY FOR A KILL OR A LAUNCHER**
        (asked 2026-11-20: "bots need to combine goals. We can't either be fighting or going for
        an item; we must be able to do both. We can run and jump in a direction while shooting in
        another direction; we just need to tend in the direction of the item we are going for.
        Sometimes an enemy is also heading for an item and will just disappear from a fight. We
        should not follow the player if we are heading for an important item that we are timing,
        like quad, RL or RA. Only commit to following a player if they are believed to be low on
        health and we can kill them, or they have the RL and we don't"). Two halves, a switch each:
        (a) THE FEET KEEP THE GOAL IN A FIGHT: the aim on the enemy, the feet (forward, side, the
        hop and its jumps) still tend along the route to the item goal; the dodge jinks only on
        the axis the route does not need (the route across the line to him: the side key is the
        route's and the jinks are toward and away; the route along the line: the side key
        jinks); a goal straight behind the line to him is the one case the feet cannot serve both
        (no back key unless chased: 0w), and there a timed key item due breaks the fight off,
        the view back on the way. Today the fight's feet are the dodge and the press at him
        (footwork.cpp), the route's keys overwritten, except under `itemfirst`. (b) THE CHASE IS
        A CHOICE: an enemy lost from sight is followed only when he is believed killable (the
        ledger's body low against the bot's might: 0q's ledger, today pain cries and pickups
        heard) or he holds the launcher and the bot does not; otherwise the bot goes on with its
        goal, always so when its goal is a timed key item (quad, launcher, red armour) due.
        TO MEASURE FIRST, people and bots by one rule (se-people `chase.*`): every fight that ends
        with the enemy gone from sight alive, whether the watcher followed (came near where he
        was last seen within a few seconds), by the enemy's health then and who held a launcher,
        and whether a kill came of it; and the speed kept in a fight (`fight-speed`).
        MEASURED (2026-11-20, labs/chase_read.hpp; se-people `chase.*` and `fight-speed`, se-team
        `team.chase.*` off the server's truth with each bot's gone put down to its mind then:
        `cause.{chase,fight,goal-that-way,elsewhere}`): dm2, people's 95 recordings against six
        10-minute bot matches. Fights a minute even (65 / 65); ended with the enemy gone alive 40 /
        41 %; FOLLOWED people 30 %, bots 37.5; people follow a low enemy (body under 80, the
        server's truth) no more than a healthy one (28 / 30 %: they cannot see it) and one with the
        launcher a little more (32); a kill within 6 s after following 35 % (left 29), the bots
        28 (23). The fight's speed even (289 / 293), its closing speed people 59, bots 33.
        BUILT: `chasekill` (fighter.cpp: a lost or heard enemy followed only when the ledger says
        killable, body 60 or less with the bot at 100 or more, or he was heard with the launcher or
        the arc and the bot holds neither) and `goalfeet` (footwork.cpp: in a fight the route's
        keys under the aimed view kept: across the line the side key the route's and the press
        jinking toward and away; toward him the forward the route's and the dodge jinking the
        side; a goal behind the line keeps the fight's feet, counted). Both judging.
        `chasekill`: holds 25.7k thinks a match, followed 37.5 -> 36.4 % (it is not the chase
        mode that follows), frags a minute 14.6 -> 13.5; head to head (12 seeds, base +3.3) red
        with it +0.4, blue -2.3: +1.35 a match, even. `goalfeet`: a match's fight commands 29.1k
        across, 15.7k ahead, 7.9k behind; frags a minute 14.6 -> 11.3, fights ended gone 41 -> 55 %,
        followed 38.6 % (unchanged), the closing speed 33 -> 24 (people 59: further off),
        deaths a minute 2.36 -> 1.94 (people 3.13); HEAD TO HEAD red with it +3.6, blue -4.5: +4.0
        A MATCH, a clear win.
        THE CAUSE, read 1.5 s after the enemy was last seen (a second past the fight's beat): of
        the bots' gones 20 % in chase mode (followed 38 %), 40 % fighting again (another enemy or
        him again: 46 %), 13 % with the goal that way (39 %), 27 % going elsewhere (29.5 %): THE
        MEASURE'S FLOOR is ~30 % (a bot going elsewhere still nears the spot), so people (30) barely
        follow, and the bots' excess is renewed fights, not chases. (A first read at the gone itself
        put 67 % on "fight": the beat had not yet been thought again.) `goalfeet` out of sight does
        nothing (the bot is not aiming then: the route has the feet). VERDICTS (12 seeds, both
        sides): `goalfeet` +4.0 on dm2, +3.45 on e1m2; `goalfeet` with `chasekill` +3.45 on dm2 (+4.4
        red, +2.5 blue); `chasekill` alone +1.35. BOTH ON in the preset (the maintainer's rules; the
        follow share a step toward people's, the fights' pace further off: frags a minute 14.6 ->
        ~13). `breakoff` (the fight dropped for a timed item due) -11.4: dropped (below). NEXT: the
        fights' pace (people's closing speed 59 against the bots' 24 with `goalfeet`: people press
        in AND keep their way; read whether people's closing is toward their goal), then 0q's
        ledger (own hits counted) so `chasekill`'s "killable" is a real belief.
        THE PACE READ (session 43, se-people `fight.<mine>-vs-<his>.*`: the fight's closing by
        both launchers loaded): people's closing with the launcher is the bots' (36 / 40); without

      - **7, turning in mid-air** to face an enemy behind, a jump spent turning round to shoot.
      - **8, bunny hopping backwards, and map knowledge by skill**.
      - **9, the warm-up show**: before the match the bots hop (backwards too) and rocket jump
        round the map.
      - **10, firing with an enemy in view** (people 37 %, bots 24 %): what is left after facing.
      - **11, the goal engine**: the catalogue's needs and horizons acting, short goals beside a
        long one; written strategies for e1m2, dm2, dm3, dm4, dm6, cmt3b, cmt5, aerowalk, ztndm3.
      - **12, combos for experts**: knocked up and finished in the air (a blast at the feet, then
        the rocket or the arc), the double in the face under the quad.
      - **13, prediction by ping for every weapon**: hitscan and, for intermediates and experts,
        the launchers by distance; bots must not play alike at a high ping.
      - **14, dm2's moves**: the two-player stack to the quad, rides on moving platforms, the
        grenade-and-rocket combo onto the walkway, the rocket-jump tricks refreshed.
      - **15, speed and firepower**: speed and a loaded launcher (people 32 % of their time);
        most of the speed gap is the hop (1b).
      - **16, adapting to opponents** during a match, from the live feed, then on their own.
      - **17, the final test**: a 4on4 pickup with people on a live server, the bots' talk in and
        out, and what it teaches.
      - **18, HOW PEOPLE FIGHT, COPIED** (asked 2026-11-20: "study how people fight to do scripted
        moves: jumping, centring the view, avoiding rockets, strafing to avoid being hit"). Read
        already (se-people): the fight's feet across and along the line to the enemy, by range and
        with the launcher (`fight.*`), the keys in a fight (`keys.fight.*`), the hand's sweeps.
        TO MEASURE FIRST, people and bots by one rule off recordings (se-people `fight.*` grown,
        a fight read cut into instances as se-recipes cuts hop starts): (a) jumps a fight-minute
        and when (on a rocket's coming, at a corner, to close), the height and the speed kept;
        (b) the view on the enemy: its angle off him over a fight, how fast it closes after he
        appears (the centring), how often the view leads him; (c) EVERY ROCKET passing a player
        (its flight against his box): how near it burst or passed, the damage, and what he did
        in the 0.3 s before (jumped, turned his side key, stopped, ran on), by distance at
        launch: the dodge that works, and how often; (d) the strafe's rhythm in a fight (the
        side's hold time, people's against the dodge's jinks) and the hit rate it costs the
        shooter. Then copy the moves that work whole (recipes.hpp's way: people's keys and view
        played back through the move, proved, chosen by the state) and judge each by the hits it
        takes and head to head.
      - **18b, THE LAUNCHER FIGHT AS PEOPLE FIGHT IT** (asked 2026-11-20: "Quake duels and fights
        with the RL are jumping and strafing (this is dodging) in random patterns and trying to
        fire at the enemy's feet to launch him into the air or just give him blast damage.
        Shooting at the enemy risks missing completely, but firing at the ground near him still
        hurts. Once he is in the air, a timed rocket or grenade in the face, if we are good:
        experts do that. With the quad, the SSG kills when close. The LG is very good for this
        too: shoot him up into the air and finish him with the LG"). The parts, each a switch:
        (a) THE FEET: the rocket aimed at the floor under him at every range a burst there
        reaches him (today the feet only past 400 units, the middle nearer: kRocketNear), his
        middle only when he is in the air; (b) THE DANCE: jumps and strafes in a random rhythm
        while the launcher fights (today the dodge strafes only, never jumps), off people's
        rhythm (item 18 (a), (d)); (c) THE AIR FINISH, experts first: an enemy knocked into the
        air (or jumping) is finished before he lands: the lightning gun when loaded and in its
        reach, else a rocket or a grenade timed on his arc at his body (the lead's arc exists:
        `target_arc`); item 12's combo; (d) THE QUAD'S SCATTERGUN: under the quad, the
        double-barrelled scattergun at close range (a quad blast at that range hurts the firer
        too). TO MEASURE FIRST, people and bots by one rule off recordings: where each rocket's
        burst lands against the nearest enemy (his body, the floor at his feet, a wall, wide), the
        knock-ups (a player thrown up by a burst he did not fire) and what follows before he lands
        (another burst, the beam, a death), the weapon each kill was made with and whether the
        victim was in the air, the kills with the quad by weapon, and the jumps a fight-minute.
        MEASURED (2026-11-20, labs/blast_read.hpp, se-people `blast.*`, `knock.*`, `death.*`,
        `jumps.*`; dm2, people's 95 recordings against six 10-minute bot matches): THE GAP IS THE
        LAUNCHERS' USE, NOT THE DANCE. Jumps a fight-minute even (people 37, bots 40). Bursts put
        to a player a player-minute: people 4.2, bots 1.2; of those at the floor by his feet 21 /
        28 %, on the body 1 / 1.5, with him in the air 66 / 53. Knock-ups a minute 0.94 / 0.18
        (followed by another burst before he lands 13 / 11 %, dead before landing 12 / 10). A
        death's last blow a burst 44 / 14 % (the bots' 75 % "other": nails, a fall, unseen), in
        the air 38 / 35 %; under the quad a burst 53 / 16 %, pellets 9 / 9 %. Fire a
        player-minute (the weapons' sounds): the rocket launcher 8.2 / 2.35, the grenade
        launcher 1.28 / 0.007, the nailgun 1.07 / 5.4; held: the launcher 46 / 25 % of the time,
        the grenade launcher 16 / 0 %. Per minute holding the launcher, rockets fired 17.8 / 9.3.
        NEXT, in order: (1) why the finger holds with the launcher up (the trigger's reasons
        counted: out of reach, off the view, `blastsafe`, a mate in line, no one in sight) and
        fire at the floor under him as people do; (2) the grenade launcher taken and fired

        THE FINGER READ (2026-11-20, session 43; bots/fighter.hpp `FingerWhy`, se-team
        `team.finger.*`, se-people `rl.*`, `held.*`, `rockets-per-owned-min`; six 10-minute dm2

        weapon round corners. BUILT, JUDGED TOO RARE, KEPT OFF (bots/launcher_fire.hpp): `earlob`
        (one heard or lost a moment ago with no straight line to him: the arc's pitch, the grenade
        flown through the world, thrown when its first impact lands within 120 of him) throws
        0.005 a bot-minute; `lobtake` (the lob worth a third of the launcher as a goal to one
        carrying the launcher) moved the lob carried with the launcher 2.6 -> 2.8 % (people 29):
        head to head with both -0.8 over 12 seeds, noise. The lob is not carried, so nothing fires
        it. NEXT for the lob: find what keeps it off the goals (the decision log's `item_goal`
        candidates with the launcher carried near the mega), then `earlob` again.
        THE LOB AS A GOAL (decision log): it scores 10-27 (its worth over the time to it) against
        the 50-90 chosen, even at a third of the launcher's worth: people take it passing the mega
        and the quad, which they take twice as often (the mega 1.06 / 0.48 a minute): the lob
        follows item control, not its own worth. `aimclear` (the aim's height raised to his middle
        or chest when its line bursts by the shooter) judged: 1.9 commands a bot-minute, the
        holds on the aim's point 23.5 -> 23.2 %, head to head +2.5 in the noise: those holds are
        the LEAD's (his way ahead runs into the door frame), not the height's. Off.
        A CORRECTION (session 43): people's dm2 recordings are 2on2; this item's bot sets above
        (and the MEASURED baseline before them) were 4on4, so per-player numbers (the launcher
        carried, bursts and deaths a player-minute) compared unlike games. LIKE FOR LIKE (12
        ten-minute 2on2 expert matches, `se-team --size=2`, today's preset; people / bots): the
        launcher carried 57 / 41 %; rockets a minute carrying it 19.3 / 7.8; loaded, one in view
        55 / 37, none in view 11.6 / 2.3; of the rockets in view 53 / 76 %, just lost 24 / 16,
        blind 23 / 9; by range near 54 / 18, mid 65 / 48, far 46 / 44; the spells taken at a
        launcher's spot 0.77 / 0.36 a player-minute, kept 38 / 51 s; from a pack 0.27 / 0.16;
        an enemy in plain view 23 / 18 % of the time; deaths 2.9 / 1.4 a player-minute; the
        fights' closing 59 / 37 (without the launcher on one with it 111 / 49); bursts put to a
        player 5.0 / 1.5 a player-minute. The finger's gap stands (larger); the carry's narrows;
        THE PACE is the 2on2's gap: people's games twice as deadly. ITS CAUSE, read (se-people
        `area.*`, `show.*`, people / 2on2 bots): people hold the armours' areas (YA held 55 / 32
        %, RA 67 / 41, MH 58 / 19; contested 23-26 / 7-9 %) and take each as it shows: the RA
        1.93 / 0.55 a minute, taken 3.6 / 61 s after showing (p50); the mega 1.06 / 0.31, 7.8 /
        39 s; the quad 1.1 / 24 s; the launcher 1.5 / 31 s; enemies come into view 20.3 / 12.6
        a minute. WHAT THE BOTS DO AS A KEY ITEM SHOWS (se-team `shows.tsv`, six 2on2 seeds, 310
        showings): the red armour has a bot going for it at half its showings and still lies 58 s
        (p50; 73 s with none going); the mega has one going at 3 of 27 and lies 50 s; the yellow
        armours almost never (2 of 115). The bots' goals then are THE QUAD: of the bots not going
        for a showing armour, most were going for the quad (roam), then the other red armour and
        the launcher. In 2on2 the quad is a sink for every bot's goal while people hold the
        armours' areas and time the quad. NEXT: why the quad holds them (the team's roles, the
        quad's due: the decision log's `role` and `item_goal` with its options), and the armours
        timed as people time them.
        18b WHERE IT STANDS (session 43): ON `rlback`, `afterfire`, `earfire`, `itemz`; rockets a
        minute carrying the launcher on recordings 8.2 -> 10.3 (people 19.3). What is left of the
        in-view gap (34 against 55 a minute) is the maintainer's near rule (no burst under 160:
        the scattergun under 192, 57 % of that band) and SIGHTING (an enemy in plain view the bot
        has not taken in: `weapon` 15 %, `unseen`/`none` 12 % of the loaded in-view samples). (c)
        THE AIR FINISH and (d) THE QUAD'S SCATTERGUN show no gap of their own: after a knock-up
        another burst 13 / 11 %, dead before landing 12 / 10 %; under the quad pellets 9 / 9 % of
        the deaths: the knock-ups' count (0.94 / 0.18) follows the fire rate. Open: the lob's
        carry (item control), the hold on the lead.
      - **19, THE THOUGHTS OVER THEIR HEADS** (asked 2026-11-20: "a debug mode for the bots
        that adds their thoughts above their heads. It should record into mvd demos as well.
        Only supported by our client of course. Maybe call them mvdd or something. This would
        also let you see their thoughts in the demo but also let me, by watching a demo. It
        should also convert to a video if I want to."). A TOOL ITEM, for the maintainer and the
        agent alike:
        (a) THE MODE: a switch on our server and in `se-team` (`--thoughts`; a cvar for a live
        server) that has every bot publish, each think, a few short lines of what it has in mind,
        off the decisions it already records (`ai::Why`, the decision log's rows): its mode and
        its target, the item goal and why, the role, the trigger's reason (`FingerWhy`), the
        feet's note (press, goalfeet, dodge), what it heard. Recording decides nothing: a match
        with the mode on plays bit for bit as one with it off (a test holds the digests equal).
        (b) THE RECORDING: the thoughts written into the match's demo as our own data, read by
        our client only: A TRACK of the replay file version 2 (the maintainer, 2026-11-20:
        "maybe store it as a track then"; docs/tv.md: the format has tracks for this), a
        `thoughts` track beside the match's own, so a reader that does not know it skips it;
        one command writes the plain `.mvd` for other clients. Recorded in docs/replays.md.
        (c) THE PICTURE: our client draws each bot's lines over its head while the replay plays
        (in the window, `-playdemo`), the text scaled by distance and faded behind walls, a key
        to show or hide them and to pick one bot's thoughts in full; `make bot-video
        ARGS="--thoughts"` renders the video with them (`tools/bot-match-video.sh`), and
        `make bot-why` can point at a moment of it.
        Yardstick: a 10-minute match's replay with the track plays in our client with every bot's thoughts
        on screen and in step with the decision log (`se-why` at the same time names the same
        mode and goal); its size against the plain demo's; the plain `.mvd` stripped from it
        is byte for byte the one recorded without the mode.
    - **Tried and dropped** (so it is not tried again blind):
      - (2026-11-20, experts, dm2, 12 ten-minute seeds, on top of `goalfeet`) `breakoff`: a timed
        key item of its own due (`itemfirst`) and the enemy over 100 degrees off its way and not
        coming on: not aimed at, the view on the way (the retreat's front-only run, bot.cpp
        `run_front_only`). Head to head red with it -15.0, blue -7.7: -11.4 A MATCH. A back turned
        on a fight for an item is shot; the fight's feet toward the item (`goalfeet`) is the way.
      - (2026-11-14, experts, six ten-minute dm2 seeds) `hopclear`: the hop's aim along the route
        pulled back (the look halved) until the box reaches it straight at its own height:
        flights at 380+ ending in a wall 59 -> 62 %, speed +1, frags a minute 13.9 -> 12.2.
        `airhold`: the side key held in the hop's air where the air's sets let go of every key,
        unless it brakes: no-key commands 43 -> 27 %, walls 59 -> 63 %, speed -2.6. The hop's
        wall look (`hop_see_s`) 0.12 -> 0.25 and 0.4 s: walls 60 and 58 %, speed +1.9 and +1.0.
        None moved the walls: the corner is met with the turn already spent.
      - (2026-11-10) `hopkeep`: past 380 u/s the expert's hop steered as the simple hop (turning
        only as the route asks): six 3-minute dm2 seeds, speed 182 against 192 without it, hop
        share 12 % against 14 %, fast flights crashing 64 % against 59 %: the wide weave is not
        what crashes them; dropped. `lane` off: bumps 20.9 against 20.5 a bot-minute, no part.
      - The weave planned per flight (`weave`): wall stops 135 -> 230, speed 183 -> 164; the
        bots' weave already matched people's, the gap is the speed.
      - Headroom by a predicted path (straight, an arc, along the route): each missed the lintel
        or held jumps for nothing; the ceiling over the route's nodes held.
      - `clearhop` (a hop's jump flown along the route first): crashes unchanged, chains
        shorter; the simple hop's side chosen a whole flight ahead, or swapped in mid-air: noise.
        An average on the expert's side-key hop crashed more (its hands wander).
      - The watch widened to the frontier (0j's first try): head to head -24 and -48, sighting
        59.7 -> 56.7 %: the nearest frontier nodes are corners, not the doorways people watch.
      - Every locked door resting shut kept off the routes: `no route` 56-83 % on dm2.
      - The lost rule taking a node walked to then fallen to (dm2's door top): no help.
      - Curving every hop's first jump, straights too: crashes 35 %, frags a minute 7.9.
      - A dodge shaped like people's (sides held 0.7-1.2 s): neutral; people's speed across the
        line is not the side key's length.
      - A lead with one intercept pass, or with the arc dropped: neither outside the noise.
      - A calm view along the motion: +4 over 12 seeds.
      - Water priced 2.5x, later 2x and 4x, in the route search: wet 38 -> 34 % and 25 -> 24 %,
        head to head both ways: the wet time is the fight in the water, not the routes' choice.
      - In water, the wish made level and full: wading speed unchanged.
      - Rising to the surface on a pool's walk: dithering, wet 35 -> 52 %.
      - `retry` (a missed jump cut 30 s, an unreachable goal let be): YA lying unchanged, frags
        down; retried jumps fall again.
      - `pits` (jumps over a pool a miss drops into cut): YA lying unchanged; the ways round are
        longer.
      - Packs fetched whenever calm (first `packinfer` builds): the YA lay 73 -> 85 %, head to
        head -5, then -37 and -33.
      - Keeping the trigger down through a sway past its width: fire in view +2.5 points only.
      - A view fixed on a sound heard: it stopped the scan (the swipe and back replaced it).
      - Backing off to cover out of sight first: cowards died more crossing open ground.
      - Short goals for shells and nails: the detours cost speed and frags.
      - The turn back after a lost enemy at 180 degrees a second: -0.9 frags a minute.
      - Holding an armour from a spot that sees it: it still lay 76 %; who goes matters, not
        where the holder stands.
      - The hand's turn-back rule on the aim too: accuracy 30.1 %, dropped for the aim.
- **M18r5, `ge bot run / compare / sweep`**, with `ge bot nav` beside them: a change to a bot
  shown before and after in frags, deaths, items and humanness.
- **M18r6, bots over a link**: the brain behind a client's packets on a slot M18f's server
  reserves, filling M18q's lobby.
